# TV DIABLO — BLUEPRINT

GENERATED by `tv/blueprint.py` from the code in this tree. Do not hand-edit: regenerate it.
A hand-written map is out of date the first time anyone edits anything, and the version that
is confidently wrong is worse than no map at all.

    generated 2026-11-03 16:52

## THE REVERSE-BLUEPRINT RULE (inherited from ~/achilles-revival)

> Normal blueprint = design first, build after.
> Reverse-blueprint = audit what exists, map it truthfully, fix what's wrong.
> **NEVER guess. ALWAYS trace.**

Trace, then change. In one session this rule killed three of my own confident diagnoses:
the reel size was not the lag, the PNG encoder was not on the primary path, and the tooltip
cropper was not starved of readers. Each was plausible and each was wrong, and the
measurement is what said so.

## THE ARCHITECTURE — why every reading is read twice

Konyo, after a defect I reported turned out not to be one: "this is exactly why LAWS and
engines are built and secondary accuracy verifiers need and are built — whatever is gapped
needs to be added and integrated ... we need it properly architectured".

THE CASE THAT PROVES IT. A register said an item sat at loc=floor. I called that a WHERE
defect and said it belonged in his stash. Rendering the frame showed RUNE GRIP / RING as a
FLOOR DROP LABEL in the Rogue Encampment — no tooltip, no stash. The reader was right and
the reviewer was wrong, and only a SECOND, DIFFERENT look settled it.

## GAME-DATA SOURCE — the 28GB D2R CASC store (footprints, art, tables)

The authoritative source for item inventory FOOTPRINTS (invwidth x invheight), base-item data,
unique/set tables and HD art is the LOCAL Diablo II Resurrected CASC store on his Mac — never
memory and never a guess. His rule for #8 (wrong item sizes): "from DATA, never memory".

- CASC store (28GB, TVFS), via the CrossOver bottle (exact path in private memory d2r-casc-toolchain-rebuild):
    ~/CXPBottles/Battle.net Desktop App/drive_c/Program Files (x86)/Diablo II Resurrected
- Extractor (in-repo): tv/casc_extract.c ; build in tv/extract_ui_icons.py _need_toolchain(). The /tmp
  CascLib toolchain does NOT survive — rebuild per that memory (CascLib + g++, needs -DCMAKE_POLICY_VERSION_MINIMUM=3.5).
- Access files BY PATH with a data: prefix (TVFS, no listfile):
    data:data/global/excel/armor.txt | weapons.txt | misc.txt | itemtypes.txt | uniqueitems.txt | setitems.txt
    data:data/local/lng/strings/item-names.json   (enUS display names)
- Footprints: armor/weapons/misc.txt columns code, invwidth, invheight; uniques/sets map index -> code -> base dims.
- Extracted dims are cached in-repo at tv/footprints.json so a build need not re-extract every time.

So nothing here trusts a single reading. Four independent kinds of check, and each answers a
question the others cannot:

    LAW          needs no evidence at all. The Horadric Cube is furniture whether or not
                 anything has ever seen it. tv/inventory_law.py
    EVIDENCE     needs repetition, and refuses to conclude from thin data. Wilson, not a raw
                 ratio: 3-for-3 scores 0.438, not 1.00. tv/confidence.py, six lanes
    CONTRADICTION  two engines that must agree, asserted continuously. When they part, THAT
                 is the finding — never averaged. tv/corroborate.py
    A SECOND READ  the same frame read a second way, graded on WHAT it named, WHERE it
                 placed it and HOW it read it. tv/retro_gate.py

And one rule underneath all four: an UNMEASURED thing stays unmeasured. `None` is not `0`.
"nobody looked" and "we looked and found nothing" are opposite facts, and every ledger
here keeps them apart.

## THE ENGINE — every module, what it does, and what a reader gets wrong

Grep a CONCEPT here and land on the module that owns it. That is the whole job, and it is
asserted by a gate rather than hoped for: `footprint`, `occupancy`, `names_loc`, `terror
zone`, `slot identity` and `lattice` all scored ZERO hits in this document the day before
this section existed, while every one of them was a shipped, tested behaviour.

DERIVED every render: which modules exist, and which are imported by another module.
CURATED in `tv/engine_index.json`: purpose, territory, gotcha, entry points — a sentence
about what a module MEANS cannot be re-derived from its AST. The drift between the two is
printed below rather than hidden, because a map that quietly drops a module is the exact
failure this section was written about.

    277 modules · 1 unindexed · 0 stale entries

    ⚠ UNINDEXED — on disk with no entry, so this map does NOT describe them:
        machine_sync.py

`no importer` means no OTHER MODULE IN tv/ IMPORTS IT — measured by parsing, never by
grepping a name that may be sitting inside a comment. It is not `dead`: a CLI tool, a
hook and a routine are all reached without an import, so it is a question, not a verdict.

### capture-vision (22)

- **capture_stamp.py** — What a NEW reel may record about the window it was filmed from (route, windowLabel, w, h, os, dpi), from the finder's pin and the Windows capture half's cap_target.json; tv_diablo._capture_for_seal stamps it on the reel it seals.
      ↪ from_measured · from_index · beside_frame · os_of_this_process
      ⚠ A field that was not measured is ABSENT, never a default: dpi is copied only when dpiMeasured is True, w and h travel together, and reading an OLD index back never fills a route. Only stash_eye (surfaces.py prep_stash_grid) reads the stamp today; slot_identity's grid geometry does not, and the ALT panel band was never measured (AUDIT_B 67ec5b26).
- **chronicle_calibrate.py** — Reads the Chronicle completion BAR's gold fill from captured frames and sets it beside the board's own tally to catch calibration drift.
      ↪ bar_fill · read_reel · verdict · main
      ⚠ The fill is a WATCHDOG calibrated to about ±1.5 points, never the exact percentage — it exists to catch a 3-point disagreement (0.8395 measured against a printed 85%), so quoting it as the figure is the misuse its own docstring forbids. It is structural pixel measurement, no OCR and no model call.
- **chronicle_crop.py** — The ONE Chronicle list-band crop that BOTH reading lanes use, and it stamps onto the page which framing each lane actually read.
      ↪ list_crop · crop_answer_refused
      ⚠ It does NOT claim the crop reads better — v1780 measured crop 5/6 vs full 0/6, and v1829 measured one frame the crop refused twice that the FULL frame read fine. Both measurements stand and the transience is still open; the point is that both lanes get the SAME pixels and the framing is recorded. The band numbers live in chronicle_template — this file must never carry a second copy. Writes only to a temp file and degrades to the full frame on any failure.
- **chronicle_hunt.py** — The focused re-hunt: for a HELD grail name, brackets where its Chronicle row must lie in OTHER reels and re-reads only those frames to earn a cross-reel witness.
      ↪ hunt · targets_for · page_names
      ⚠ It deliberately does NOT hunt neighbouring frames in the SAME reel — witnesses() returns a SET, so a 7th frame in a reel already tagged cross-frame changes nothing and the name stays held forever. Aiming at other reels is the whole point (cross-reel = independent pixels). `read_page` is INJECTED, so this module never decides how a page is read; `targets_for` defaults to ledger="uniques", so a sets hunt must pass kind ending in "sets" or it searches the wrong evidence (v1917 bug). Bounded at MAX_PER_NAME=18 frames per name, and it breaks after the FIRST hit.
- **chronicle_template.py** — Locked pixel geometry of the CENTERED Chronicle modal — colour-fraction bands per tab — resolved to uniques/sets/runewords with a stated why.
      ↪ detect · resolve_tab · geometry_signals · canonical_tab · ledger_kind_for_tab
      ⚠ The v1690 docstring still says the three consumers are DELIBERATELY NOT WIRED and to 'expect zero hits' — that is stale: chronicle_retro, control_app, corroborate, health_engine, lane_lock and chronicle_crop all import it today. Also `detect_from_ocr_lines()` exists but is a WEAK SECONDARY that must never be the classify source (full-frame OCR returns the tooltip's text, not the panel chrome). Its tab vocabulary is intentionally its own — it says `sets`/`uniques` where route_totals says `set` and lane_lock says `uniques`; one_name.py exists to reconcile the three. Bands were measured only on the 2940x1912 calibration film; other aspects go through `_scale_band_for_aspect`, labelled derived-not-measured, and `signals['band_branch']` records which branch ran.
- **fake_claude.py** — A stand-in `claude` binary speaking just enough stream-json for the vision worker's TDD; TV_FAKE_MODE picks ok / slow (timeout) / junk (non-JSON).  ·  *no importer*
      ⚠ Not an importable API — it is a BINARY spoken to over a pipe, and its FILENAME is a contract: tv_diablo.py:3265 disarms the paid-subscription circuit by string-matching `fake_claude` in the binary blob, so renaming this file silently arms real billing during tests. It also has two argv shapes: without `--input-format` it answers one-shot and exits, otherwise it loops on stdin.
- **hover_calibration.py** — Records mini auto's (planned frame target, screen point) pairs while it hovers and derives the tooltip->cell offset slot_identity refuses without.
      ↪ record_step · calibrate · steps
      ⚠ It drives NOTHING — hover_mode moves the pointer and this is a passive witness of it, and record_step swallows its own errors so it can never strand his cursor. With no steps recorded calibrate() returns None and says why rather than emitting a plausible constant, which would place every item in the wrong cell with total confidence.
- **hover_drive.py** — The only file that moves his pointer: hovers slot_identity targets to reveal tooltips, building kCGEventMouseMoved and nothing else.
      ↪ preflight · walk · can_actually_move · cursor_pos · d2r_window_rect
      ⚠ There is NO click by CONSTRUCTION, not by setting — no mouse-down/up event is ever built, so no flag or future caller can make it press (a left click on an occupied D2R cell picks the item up). It aborts the moment the pointer drifts more than 6px from where it left it, because his hand on the mouse wins, and it converts each target through screen_point() one at a time so a dragged window cannot leave a stale batch of coordinates.
- **hover_mode.py** — Joins the hover PLAN to the pointer ACTUATOR: sweeps each occupied stash cell so the game renders its tooltip. It moves only — it never clicks.
      ↪ start · stop · status · plan
      ⚠ It exists because both ends already existed and nothing joined them — `hover_drive.walk()` had ZERO callers, so the button he was looking for did not render. Four stop conditions, three of them his: if HIS HAND touches the mouse the sweep aborts and the pointer is deliberately NOT restored (fighting him for it is worse than leaving it). `status()` returning running:False with stopped:None means it has NEVER RUN — a different fact from 'ran and finished', and the UI must distinguish them. start() refuses a second concurrent sweep.
- **live_miss_audit.py** — Reads the session journal and names WHICH of five links broke when a stash went unread: A no panel, B no tab named, C no intake fired, D empty, E worked.
      ↪ load · audit · _fix_for
      ⚠ It does not test the readers and never touches a camera — it only replays what the journal already recorded, and is built to run on ANOTHER machine's sessions.jsonl (that is where the cousin's failure lives). A stashTab of `""` and a VAULT tab both count as 'not identified as a tally tab', so B fires on both; only runes/gems/materials are tally tabs.
- **prove_real_intake.py** — On-demand proof that the SUBSCRIPTION intake lane really reads pixels — a golden JPG through intake_local.mjs, asserted against frozen golden names.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ main
      ⚠ Deliberately excluded from CI, pre-push and tv-tests, and imported by nothing — it costs a real vision call and needs the Mac's claude login. Exit 2 is SKIP, not a pass. It talks to the node runner DIRECTLY so the control proxy's fallback cannot fake-green it, and it fails on OVER-read (a vocab dump) as well as under-read.
- **recorder_lease.py** — One lease file lets the recorder and the reel-cutter outlive their console, and lets a restarted console adopt them instead of starting a second.
      ↪ claim · release · read · adoptable · agent_hold · pid_alive
      ⚠ A lease whose pid is dead is free; two recorders at once once killed dwm.exe, so a claim never runs beside a live holder.
- **retro_gate.py** — Wraps any retro lane's reader so it ALSO reads the surfaces-prepared (crop + 3x enlarge) frame, compares the two answers, and banks the agreement so the pair earns a Wilson score.
      ↪ gated · grade_three · bank · report · corroborate_location
      ⚠ It is a GRADER, NOT A CENSOR — the wrapped reader always returns the ORIGINAL reader's answer; a disagreement is recorded, never silently substituted, or downstream names become unattributable. It must NEVER raise into a sweep (an exception would cost a whole reel), so every failure path returns the original answer with a reason. "Could not ask" (prepared frame unavailable, or the second read came back empty) is UNKNOWN and does NOT count in the Wilson denominator. Its imports resolve at CALL time, not at module import, so a fixture root is honoured.
- **slot_identity.py** — SLOT IDENTITY — WHERE an item was seen, not just THAT it was seen: the measured stash/inventory/cube lattice turned into a cell, a slot key and a hover point; footprint() refuses an item overhanging the grid rather than clipping it.
      ↪ footprint(col,row,w,h,container) · cell_of(point,panel_box,container) · point_of_cell() · panel_box_for() · next_target()
      ⚠ GRIDS and the panel boxes are MEASURED pixel constants (inventory origin x1791 y984, cell 86.85px at 2940x1912), not tuning knobs; six equipment slots are REFUSED rather than interpolated, and nothing in this module moves a cursor.
- **stash_eye.py** — Is a panel open, and WHICH tab: the dark-gridline lattice (lattice_says), the gem/OCR tab fingerprints and classify_stash_grid, fused into one tab verdict per frame.
      ↪ lattice_says(dark_cols,frac_gear) · classify_stash_grid() · fuse_tab_signals() · analyze_frame() · tab_from_gem()
      ⚠ A dark PICTURE is not a lattice — before the contrast rule a dark scene scored dark_cols 31-40 and satisfied 'grid lattice present' as easily as a real panel (7-14); crops are aspect-pinned to his Mac's range.
- **stash_grid_score.py** — Scores stash_eye.classify_stash_grid against the hand-labelled frames in stash_grid_truth.json, with a ratchet against the last score.
      ↪ score · tally_score · load_truth · main
      ⚠ Frame rotation ate 7 of 14 corpus entries and every one was a `panel: False` NEGATIVE, so the ratchet FALSE_TALLIES=0 was being measured against zero cases that could fail it — hence the fallback to frames/corpus/<name with / flattened to __>, which reel_retention's reel_* glob does not prune. A MISSING frame must never read as a pass. It spends no vision and writes nothing; its ratchet floor lives in test_stash_eye_aspect.py, not here.
- **surface_precision.py** — What a read name is WORTH given its lane and surface — a measured Wilson lower bound per (lane, surface) that sets how many witnesses it needs and whether a paid read is worth spending there.
      ↪ precision · witnesses_required · worth_paying_to_read · recount
      ⚠ precision() returns None for 'not established' and that is NEVER 0.0 — below MIN_SAMPLES a zero means nobody looked. Unmeasured combinations still get READ ('that is how they become measured'); only a combination MEASURED over a real sample to yield nothing is refused. Two counter-intuitive measured facts live here: the ocr lane produced ONE real name in 1,136 placed sightings (it earns its keep on STRUCTURE, not names), and a CHRONICLE PAGE is the BEST naming surface there is (45 real of 74) — a gate built on 'chronicle = bad' would have discarded his best source, because precision and PROVENANCE are orthogonal and a chronicle page lists items he does not own. The table's own history is a trap: REG-439's 'correction' re-queried a ROTATED sessions.jsonl holding a fifth of the ring (REG-440); recount() exists so the table cannot rot.
- **surfaces.py** — 7 in-game surfaces in one table: stash/runes/gems/materials/inventory (stash_eye, left-anchor), chronicle-uniques/-sets (chronicle_template, centre modal), each with its enlarge and tooltip need.
      ↪ SURFACES · surface() · readers() · techniques_for() · prepare()
      ⚠ Techniques are (name, params) tuples and never call-shaped text: tv/test_reachability.py counts that shape to find functions with no caller, so a declaration written as a call made an orphan read as alive — and the warning about it tripped the same scanner when it quoted the bad example.
- **tooltip_crop.py** — Finds the tooltip rectangle on a hover run by differencing consecutive frames, and crops it, so the receipt that NAMED an item can be shown beside the item.
      ↪ changed_rect · crop_to · bounds_are_reachable · main
      ⚠ It answers WHERE the picture changed, never WHAT it says — a crop is evidence to LOOK at, not a second witness; counting it as one would double-count the single look that produced it. A diff covering more than MAX_FRAC (0.45) of the frame is a tab change or a reel cut and is REFUSED rather than cropped; below MIN_FRAC (0.002) it is JPEG noise. Unlike #31 (slot identity) this needs no calibration and no schema change — vault_retro already recorded that on a tooltip pass only the tooltip rectangle changes. Requires PIL.
- **tooltip_find.py** — Locates the D2R tooltip in one frame by OCR text density over a tile grid, returning a crop rect or a refusal, and banks a Wilson score on whether a located tooltip was really one.
      ↪ locate · density · bank · report
      ⚠ It finds the tooltip by TEXT DENSITY, not darkness and not a border — the D2R tooltip is semi-transparent with soft edges and 48.7% of his frame is near-black. Below `min_lines` it returns None deliberately (an unhovered frame and an empty one are the same honest answer). `bank(named=None)` is PENDING and counts in neither side. tooltip_find.json is live state, not a test artifact.
- **tv_diablo.py** — ON AIR autopilot (capture -> vision read -> bridge) and the READ_PROMPT contract it parses and audits: area, tz (the TERROR ZONE, read off the purple terror-zone lines), scene, stashTab, chronicleTab, names, names_loc, sockets, discovered, conf.
      ↪ READ_PROMPT / PROMPT_VER · _parse_read() -> the schema dict · --watch
      ⚠ PROMPT_VER (p1839) must bump whenever READ_PROMPT changes or older seals never get reopened; _parse_read DROPS out-of-vocabulary values into _parse_audit rather than keeping them (names_loc only equipped|inventory|stash|floor, sockets only int 1..6) — 'what the model SAID vs what survived'.
- **unify_census.py** — Prerequisite for deleting MINI (the time-boxed ONE-focus capture): AST call-graph over control_app proves every MINI technique, template and scenario exists under ON AIR; UNKNOWN blocks may_unify().
      ↪ census() · may_unify() · read_paths() · templates() · scenarios()
      ⚠ MINI AUTO (the hover technique) is deliberately out of scope — his #17/#41, reserved to him. And `reproduced=None` means nobody looked: it is not False and emphatically not True, and may_unify() refuses on it exactly as it refuses on a measured gap.

### console (6)

- **fleet_presence.py** — The one ONLINE/offline rule every Fleet surface asks: a fresh beacon or a live recorder is online, anything else says how long ago it was seen.
      ↪ presence_of · stamp_fleet · ago_words
      ⚠ Being in the site's online list is not enough: the presence key outlives a sleeping PC by its TTL.
- **launcher_decide.py** — REG-1514/1758/1940 - both launchers' one decision: bring the running console forward, leave it running, or let the launcher replace it (v1379.1's stale-code case and a windowless console only).
      ↪ decide · main
      ⚠ Never binds or kills anything: it only ASKS the console on the given port (/api/window, /api/freshness, /api/status moduleFreshness) and POSTs a front request. Exit 0 = the console came forward, the launcher ends; exit 3 = replace it (it SAYS it is older than the disk or has no window, or nothing serves the port); exit 2 = could not tell (a timeout, an ok:false or unreadable answer, a window mode it does not know, the 9 s budget ran out) - the console is left running. REG-1940: exit 1 is never a verdict, because an uncaught exception exits 1; the Windows launcher read 1 as consent and Stop-Process -Forced a fullscreen or slow console.
- **launcher_pull.py** — The launcher pull decision: a file that matches the fetched update is that update, not a local edit.
      ↪ classify · apply · main
      ⚠ Classifies against the origin/main already fetched, then resets only when a second read still says every tracked change is a plain modification whose bytes match that blob (CR ignored), HEAD is an ancestor of it, and no untracked path would be replaced. git reset --hard replaces such a file and exits 0. The added-path list uses -z and --no-renames: without -z the names arrive as one string, so an untracked file among them is missed and the reset replaces it, and without --no-renames a similar rename is hidden. A file that occupies a path the update turns into a directory is in the way only when that file is not tracked; a tracked file is the update and the reset must land. A tracked directory that holds an untracked file is in the way, because the reset deletes that directory. An ignored file inside that directory is in the way too. Names are asked as literal paths, so a bracket is not a wildcard. A symlink is not treated as a directory, so an untracked link in that place stays. A name that is not UTF-8 stops the reset. A real edit, a file in the way, a history that is not a fast-forward, or a git that cannot be read resets nothing. It does not fetch. Fetching first would make the on-disk update no longer match origin, and the pull would stay blocked.
- **loose_wires_audit.py** — Re-runnable audit of module-level definitions nothing consumes, counting registry entries and dispatch tables as joins so a registered check is not reported as an orphan.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ python3 tv/loose_wires_audit.py (a script: it runs at import, there is no main)
      ⚠ ⚠ A REGISTRY ENTRY IS A JOIN. Its first pass counted only ast.Call and reported 310 orphans, flagging every doctor check registered as a table VALUE. Corrected to 73. Some functions are also uncalled ON PURPOSE (control_app._open_board_once is protected by a test) — this tool reports candidates, it does not rule.
- **ls_wal_guard.py** — Automatic pruning of the Mac native console's WebKit LocalStorage write-ahead log (localstorage.sqlite3-wal, which reached 26 GB): wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE) + integrity_check at startup before the WebView, and a 30-minute size watch that publishes lsWal on /api/status.
      ↪ startup_prune · periodic_check · _ls_wal_loop
      ⚠ It never truncates a WAL while any process holds it: TRUNCATE holds the writer lock while waiting on WebKit's readers and a WebKit write that hits busy is a lost localStorage write, so the live 30-min look is PASSIVE only and the shrink happens at the next --open startup. Holders that lsof cannot read are UNKNOWN and skipped. A holder found at startup is a leftover from an earlier console (no WebView exists yet) and is reported, never killed.
- **relaunch_hold.py** — The relaunch INTERLOCK (his #38 ruling): hold a relaunch while a sweep is reading, then GREEN-LIGHT it by itself when the work finishes, so a sweep is never killed mid-read and the request is never silently dropped.
      ↪ new_state · hold · release · contract · stuck · note_clear
      ⚠ Pure functions over a plain dict, deliberately - control_app owns the state so the LAW can run on a GitHub runner instead of importing pywebview and a live port. ⚠ THREE TRAPS. (1) `release(ok=None)` is UNKNOWN and MUST NOT FIRE; firing then relaunches into a sweep it merely failed to see. (2) A re-ask updates the reason and MUST NOT move the deadline, or pressing the button on any timer shorter than HOLD_TTL_S makes a bounded hold unbounded. (3) The expiry is checked BEFORE the world, so a permanently-stuck sweep cannot outlive the bound.

### console-ui (9)

- **ask_view.py** — Writes .view_request.json naming ONE console pane the console should bring to front once, so a human eye can photograph it; the harness has eyes and no hands.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ ask · clear · main
      ⚠ Nothing IMPORTS it — the join is a FILE (.view_request.json), read by control_app.view_request(). It is a REQUEST, not a command: one-shot per id, refused outright while a capture is live, and refused states (STALE/UNKNOWN/HELD/BROKEN) paint nothing. PATH is bound at IMPORT time, so setting TV_VIEW_REQUEST afterwards does not move it.
- **console_snapshot.py** — Shadow mode: decides which of a console's account stores ride its signed Roster profile, when, and what a viewer is told about its age (live / offline, last sync).
      ↪ clean · due · apply_reply · record_ok · write_cache · read_cache · view · scrub_store · with_api · budget
      ⚠ Only d2r_* stores leave the PC and DENY keeps history/film/debug stores home; a string naming a disk path or install id is blanked, nothing else re-typed, so the viewer draws the same bytes. Uploads are change-only, at most once per MIN_GAP_MS (plus boot) and DAY_CAP a day, signed with the roster key; functions/api/roster.js (the profile half of the roster publish, roster:<owner>:profile) repeats the same privacy checks server side.
- **control_app.py** — The console process itself: serves :17772 (control_ui.html + ~65 /api/* routes), owns ON/OFF/SIM/STOP agent lifecycle, and runs 25 background loops.
      ↪ main · status_payload · start_agent · stop_agent · chronicle_sweep_start
      ⚠ Not an app shell. 32,388 lines / ~470 defs: the Kai OCR name-resolution engine (_kai_*), chronicle + vault sweeps, retention/prune, heart/eagle/rescue/drift loops all live HERE, not in the sibling modules named after them — 107 modules in tv/ import it as `ca`. It has NO module-level VERSION: the only stamp is the single `"ver": "v3089"` literal inside status_payload() (line 27608), bump_version.py refuses the file if it finds two, and test_control locks it to tv_diablo.VERSION.
- **css_who_wins.py** — CLI lookup over bible.html's top-level <style> rules: for a selector (and optionally one property) it lists every declaring block and says which last declaration actually wins.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ python3 tv/css_who_wins.py .hero-title color · blocks_for(path, selector)
- **extract_ui_icons.py** — Pulls the console's tab and MINI icons out of the local 28GB D2R CASC install as art/ui_*.png, replacing system emoji with true game sprites.
      ↪ main · roles_to_extract · read_sprite · best_frame · pull
      ⚠ A .sprite is a FRAME ATLAS, not one image — `total width` is frameW × frameCount, so the naive reader that worked for 611 single-frame item icons walks off the end of the buffer on the first UI sprite. UI icons are ANIMATED (3 frames to 27) and several start fully transparent, so it takes the MOST OPAQUE frame, never frame 0. Without the CascLib toolchain it exits 77 (SKIP) — a machine with no D2R install is not a failing machine; the committed PNGs are the artifact. `--check` verifies disk and extracts nothing.
- **his_answers.py** — Keeps his answers to the questions THIS console asked him (console_doctor.ASKS), one per question with the question's fingerprint, and applies them at read time so an answered question stops billing him and the row says what he chose.
      ↪ load · record · withdraw · standing · apply · entry_for
      ⚠ A changed fingerprint REOPENS the question and keeps the old answer as stale history; ruled holds 30 days, snooze 7, handoff until the question changes. An unreadable store is reported and NEVER written over. The path is chosen by the console (_decision_path), so each machine keeps its own answers.
- **live_panel_gate.py** — Grades the LIVE console's /api/status uiBeat — panel geometry (ZERO-HEIGHT / OFF-SIDE / BELOW-FOLD / ON-SCREEN) plus whether the Advanced drawer's fill and refreshers actually ran.
      ↪ check · prove · panels_of · gate_mode · main
      ⚠ BELOW-FOLD is REPORTED and never failed — his 660px console window is deliberate design, so content below the fold is normal; only ZERO-HEIGHT and OFF-SIDE are RED. A console it cannot reach is UNKNOWN (exit 2), NOT a pass. It exists because render_check.py grades a seeded FIXTURE that lays out differently from the app — at 1120x628 the gate saw taskforce at y=224 h=30 while the live console had it at y=1050 h=502; both instruments were right about different pages. The second question (advFill absent / refresher MISSING or FAILED / fleet placeholder on an open drawer) exists because a panel can be flawlessly rendered and carry nothing that was ever fetched.
- **roster_publish.py** — Builds the Fleet 3.0 roster body for its own route and KV key (hourly, change-only, plus boot), keeps the receipt of each POST, and carries each owner's signature verdict (#95) to the views, the panel and the doctor.
      ↪ budget · owner_of · roster_key · section_of · sections_from · characters_read · vault_read · stash_read · chronicle_read · reels_read · packed · hash_of · due · apply_reply · post_record · publish_outcome · view_one · view_of · write_cache · read_cache · roster_html · panel_words · signature_words · signing_verdict · doctor_row
      ⚠ An unread section is omitted, never an empty list. The key is the owner label. The publisher must not call install_identity, river_stamp.run, or grail_tally. The hash excludes asOf, so a fresh read of the same rows does not spend a write, and it walks UTF-16 code units exactly like roster.js hashOf (REG-2511: walking code points made every emoji row read CORRUPT). due() posts at boot even when unchanged; the server skips the write when its hash matches. What that heals is exact (REG-2512, REG-2516): a SIGNED boot publish the server verifies replaces the row whole, so a section this PC never sends (`owned`) is dropped; an UNSIGNED one overwrites only the sections it sends, and the rest of a planted row stays, reading NOT signature-verified. intentVerified is True, False, or None = UNKNOWN (a row published before signing), and every view, the cache file, roster_html, the doctor and the console's roster panel keep the three apart: panel_words hands the panel's buttons and the shadow bar each row's words and this PC's own line, made by the same functions as roster_html (REG-2517). publish_outcome reads THIS PC's own receipt (refused / unsigned / signed, with why). Signing itself is roster_sign.py; this file only carries what the server said. Pictures stay off the body.
- **roster_sign.py** — #95 - each PC's Ed25519 roster signing key and two signers (cryptography, pure-Python RFC 8032) that must write byte-identical signatures; sign_headers turns the posted bytes into X-Roster-Key / X-Roster-Sig.
      ↪ key_path_in · load_key · load_or_create · sign_headers · sign · public_view · key_id · pure_sign · pure_verify · pure_public · crypto_sign · crypto_public · have_cryptography · b64u · unb64u
      ⚠ The exact bytes _roster_post sends are the bytes signed; nothing is re-serialised, so Python's and JS's JSON writers never have to agree. The key file (<log root>/.roster_key.json, TV_HIST-aware, mode 0600, gitignored) is made only where no file exists: an unreadable or inconsistent one is LEFT ALONE and the publish goes unsigned with why - a lost key stays refused until Konyo deletes rosterkey:<owner> from the Mac. The seed is never logged, returned or posted (repr and view hide it). cryptography is imported at call time only - CI's runner and the installers' PCs may not have it - and the pure RFC 8032 signer runs on every signature as the corroborator (agree True/False, None when only one signer exists).

### d2jsp (1)

- **d2jsp_price_db.py** — The d2jsp price DB: every priced post the desk read, keyed once by its forum link, priced by an age-decayed weighted median with a Wilson confidence and a separate perfect tier.
      ↪ ingest · price · is_stale · merge · load · save · checks_running · fresh_refresh
      ⚠ An outlier (w 0) stays on record but never moves the price. fresh_refresh pauses while a gate or heart2 run holds the tree, so a refresh during checks says paused rather than reading.

### gates (1)

- **law_widths.py** — The one reader of TV_LAW_WIDTHS (#42): at push time heart2 --prove --push hands a width-law red-proof the viewports its defect was MEASURED to show at, and the width laws restrict every width loop to that set.
      ↪ parse · only · pick · at · label · declared · scrub · banner
      ⚠ A PROVING DEVICE, NEVER A VERDICT: run_gates.run() calls scrub() before its first gate and heart2's _run_gate drops TV_LAW_WIDTHS from every run it did not restrict itself, so the full width sweep stays the verdict of record (CI never sets it). Unset or empty means EVERY width; garbage refuses rather than silently narrowing.

### gates-proofs (42)

- **cb_node_harness.py** — The character builder's node stand-in: a localStorage + document harness with spies on the vault and the mules, the cutters that lift the CHARACTER BUILDER JS block, the CB_DB block and the board pieces it stands on out of bible.html between their own anchors, and _run(body) which executes all of it in ONE node process. Moved verbatim out of the builder law (REG-1561) so a production lane can run the shipped tooltip composition; the ten builder laws keep importing it through that law's re-exports.
      ↪ node_path · NODE · _run · _src · _between · _builder_js · _db_json · _stage · _db · HARNESS
      ⚠ NODE is resolved by env (TV_NODE), then PATH, then the known install locations - his console runs under launchd with a bare PATH, so shutil.which alone reads 'node absent' on a Mac that has node. A candidate counts only as an executable FILE (a directory is not node). None means UNKNOWN to every caller: a declared skip in a law, a receipt saying 'node absent' in the lane - never a pass. _between refuses an anchor that is not unique; the cut for the forked-name set ends at its own ']));' because the old ');' marker once ran on into LSR.
- **comment_count_gate.py** — Gate: parses bible.html for counts asserted in prose comments and fails when a claim disagrees with the value measured in the same file.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ main
      ⚠ It deliberately is NOT a fuzzy 'number near a word' check, and the restraint is most of the design: four different counts near 'set pieces' (108 / 110 / 127 / 135) are all correct at once, so a fuzzy gate would be wrong four times and get switched off. Only claims whose subject is unambiguous are checked, each against a value parsed from the same file.
- **confidence.py** — The ONE home for Wilson lower-bound + confluence scoring — how sure we are given how many looks, and how many KINDS of evidence agreed.
      ↪ wilson_lower · confluence · shadow · sharpens_with_evidence · main
      ⚠ shadow() DECIDES NOTHING by construction — it reports wouldPass/agrees and no caller may branch on it; the disagreements are the product. wilson_lower(1,1)=0.207 not 0, and only n=0 returns 0.0. confluence() is ADDITIVE and deliberately NOT capped at 1.0, and an unknown tag scores 0 rather than a default. chronicle_retro re-exports these names, so the math already living there is a re-export, not a second copy.
- **crossfamily_claude.py** — Banks a recorded Claude verdict as a cross-family attack against the locks that already cleared their attack bar.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ seat_is_dead · parse_verdict · ask_claude · bank_held · wait_and_review · main
      ⚠ It does NOT invent the look — it reads a verdict Claude wrote and banks attacks=1 only for a surface whose answer says the claim held. A seat that is signed out, over quota or silent banks NOTHING, because an unreachable eye is an empty seat and never agreement. --wait retries until 20:00 local, the window after the 19:00 renewal.
- **disk_report_wilson.py** — Attack harness proving the disk row REFUSES bogus freed-MB figures (NaN, negative, bigger than the corpus), and banks a Wilson score.
      ↪ prove · bank_into_proof_queue · main
      ⚠ Despite the name it NEVER prunes, never calls prune_once, never touches TV_AUTO_PRUNE and never measures a disk — it only attacks the REPORTER. Its earlier 24/24 was 24 identity assertions against a passthrough writer (REG-600); the proof now is that a validator stub which accepts everything scores 0/24. 'Refused' means prunedMb=None WITH a prunedWhy sentence; a bare 0 is a measurement, not a refusal.
- **fast_gates.py** — Picks the push-time gate set for run_gates --fast: the core list plus every law that is or names a changed file. A shared file, or a run_gates change outside the registry, runs every gate.
      ↪ changed · core · registry_change · select
      ⚠ Fails closed: an unknown core name, an unreadable law, a dropped registry entry or a selector error runs every gate (or that law). The import closure is NOT followed - CI and the nightly run everything.
- **frame_release_wilson.py** — Wilson evidence for the `frame.release` lock — pure sabotages proving seal_releases_frames() refuses a bare 'nothing was taken' seal.
      ↪ run · live · main
      ⚠ It banks under a NEW lock rather than under prune.arm on purpose — prune.arm is 'may the prune RUN' and vault.apply is the write door, so filing frame-granularity attacks there would move a bigger counter and prove nothing. It calls exactly one pure predicate on in-memory dicts: it never touches the seal store, apply_plan, _prune_once, os.remove/unlink/rmtree or TV_AUTO_PRUNE, and test_frame_release_wilson.py asserts that by reading this file's own source. Each entry is ONE distinct refusal idea, not a parameter sweep — five spellings of a falsy flag count as one.
- **gate_ledger.py** — Per-checkout memory of which gates passed at which commit. A later run asks git once which files moved and carries a gate whose inputs are not among them.
      ↪ Ledger · main
      ⚠ The ledger and the input map are gitignored and never a verdict of record. A missing or unreachable proven sha, a failed diff, or a gate whose inputs cannot be named runs the gate. A FAIL is not stored. GitHub CI does not consult it. CARRY is not a fresh pass and does not bank Wilson evidence.
- **gates_parallel.py** — Runs a local --full's light gates as 3 cost-balanced shards, each in its own detached worktree, then leaves the heavy and SERIAL_ONLY gates to run alone.
      ↪ plan · why_serial · run_light · free_gb
      ⚠ Falls back to serial under 4 GB free, unknown memory, or a dirty tracked tree. Shards never carry D2R_GATE_LOCK_KEY (it leaks into tree_busy). A gate that reads machine-wide state belongs in SERIAL_ONLY.
- **hover_wilson.py** — Scores the MINI/hover autopilot's four independently-failing claims — COORDINATE, ANCHOR, READ, SLOT — with a Wilson lower bound and banks them into the self-arming lock ledger.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ score · bank_into_proof_queue · wilson_lower · probe_coordinate · probe_slot
      ⚠ THE DENOMINATOR IS SABOTAGE ATTEMPTS, NOT RUNS — an always-agreeing check may be perfect or INERT (vault_autoreel_tick ran every 45s for months at 100% agreement having swept nothing), so a claim nobody tried to break scores UNPROVEN, and UNPROVEN must not read as failing. It runs on synthetic RECTANGLES, not his screen: no cursor moves, no window opens, safe in CI and safe while he plays. It is a REPORT not a gate — run_gates invokes it with -c and it has no failing mode, so it always exits 0. bank_into_proof_queue folds by (lock, kind, src) so re-running REPLACES rather than inflating n; attempts=None banks NOTHING.
- **human_eyes_gate.py** — Gate over the human-eyes LOOKED ledger: proves the loop ran end to end and no brief is OWED past its patience. 77 = declared skip.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ check · prove · gate_mode · main
      ⚠ Nothing imports it — run_gates.py:4345 registers it as Gate('human-eyes', ... '--gate'). It does NOT perform the A13 contradiction check (a LOOKED observation vs the live console) because a gate has no running console; it asserts only proven/owed/readable. For its whole early life it was registered as `--prove` ONLY, so it sabotaged fixtures and never opened his real ledger. Exit 77 = declared SKIP (no ledger on this venue), which is not a pass.
- **js_syntax_gate.py** — Loads bible.html and tv/control_ui.html in a real JS engine (Chromium via CDP, or node) and fails on any SyntaxError — one bad escape or mid-line comment blanks the whole 37k-line page.
      ↪ check · check_with_node · find_browser · browser_can_load_localhost · main
      ⚠ A hand-rolled tokenizer was tried and REJECTED — it reported 14-16 false problems on files that parse perfectly, because these pages use template literals with nested backticks, embedded HTML and regex literals a heuristic cannot tell from division. So it asks a real engine. And when no browser is available it SKIPS LOUDLY: a gate that cannot run must say so, never pass silently.
- **live_store.py** — The one door a gate goes through when it needs a store that exists only on HIS Mac (tv/chron_evidence.json, the vault accumulator, ~/d2r_ledger_backups). `missing(*paths)` names which of them are absent; `require(case, *paths, why=)` skips the case with LIVE_STORE_MARK in the reason so the skip is COUNTED rather than silent. REG-1050: eleven gates were RED on origin and GREEN here, and neither verdict was about the shipped code — they were reading his untracked machine.
      ↪ LIVE_STORE_MARK · missing · require
      ⚠ ⚠ A SKIP IS NOT A PASS, which is the whole reason the MARK is a constant and not a sentence typed per call: `test_a_live_store_skip_is_counted` greps for it and asserts the population, so a gate that quietly stops asserting still shows up as a number. ⚠ It opens nothing itself — callers hand it paths — so it is NOT a declared toucher of any store in store_owners.STORES, and it must not become one. Its module docstring names those stores in a table, which is exactly the prose that made the coupling graph report it as an undeclared writer until `_code_only` learned to strip docstrings (v3246). ⚠ `missing()` answers ABSENT, never EMPTY — a store that exists and holds nothing is a measurement and must reach the assertion.
- **lock_evidence_corroborate.py** — Checks that no self-arming source banks more sabotage attacks for a lock than its own CLAIMS tuple declares — the arithmetic that decides whether the vault may write.
      ↪ corroborate · report · covered
      ⚠ The comparison is per (SOURCE, lock), never per lock — `reel.route` legitimately shows 34 banked against 7 declared because TWO harnesses bank for it, and a per-lock total would flag healthy locks as fraudulent. It folds via SA._fold (raw _rows double-counts). `_declared` only understands a module-level CLAIMS, so the aggregate harnesses — exactly the ones that could inflate — read UNKNOWN, and SURFACES/SCOPE is a declared scope, not coverage: ask `covered()`.
- **pathguard.py** — redirect_module_path: the context manager every path-redirecting test uses to point module.attr at tmp_path and restore it.
      ↪ redirect_module_path
      ⚠ It lives here and NOT in conftest.py because run_gates.py runs each suite as a plain script on a runner that has only pillow installed — importing the helper from conftest pulled in conftest's module-level `import pytest` and broke CI for nine consecutive runs as an ERROR, not a failure. conftest re-exports it so `from conftest import redirect_module_path` still works. Setting the env var instead is a no-op: the module constant was already bound at first import.
- **pixel_witness_wilson.py** — Sabotage harness that attacks console.pixel_rescue's paint witness with his own measured window states and scores (Wilson) the right for a PIXEL verdict to trigger a window rescue.
      ↪ attacks · run
      ⚠ It reads backwards from a normal test suite: the denominator is SABOTAGES ATTEMPTED, never agreements — a wall of inputs the witness handles comfortably scores nothing, because self_arming refuses to open on those by construction. Each case scores only when the witness REFUSES to say something dangerous. Three families, and family A (a healthy window called BLANK) is weighted heaviest because that is the direction that costs him something — it would replace the window he is using. The modalShare/p99/brightShare numbers are MEASURED from his real console in both states, not invented.
- **posix_shell.py** — REG-1632 - the one resolver for a real POSIX bash, used by every law that drives a shell snippet (the pre-push port chooser, the launcher block, the push-line clock), so those laws prove on the ALT too.
      ↪ bash
      ⚠ On Windows `bash` on PATH is usually C:\Windows\System32\bash.exe - the WSL launcher, which runs nothing without a distribution (measured on the ALT). bash() never returns it; it falls back to Git for Windows' own bash beside the git a PC pulls with, else None - a law then says UNKNOWN, never a pass. Windows paths go through ntpath on every host, so its own cases run on his Mac and on CI.
- **printer_wilson.py** — Proves printer.stream() never invents an answer: five sabotages (owner raises, owner empty, nameless rows, stranger reel, printer_reach raises) must give UNKNOWN-with-a-reason across all 7 stations.
      ↪ prove() · python3 tv/printer_wilson.py --bank
      ⚠ Same 'A2 · step 1' label as sweep_wilson, different lock — this banks under printer.stream. Its own docstring still says "all five stations" while printer.STATIONS is now seven (in, funnel, template, route, extract, out, tombstone); `template` was added at v2571, which is why _stations() QUOTES P.STATIONS instead of copying it. A bare UNKNOWN with no reason does not count as a refusal.
- **prove_record.py** — REG-2353 (#42 R16) - the push-time prover's own record: every heart2 --prove ... --push appends one JSON line (per proof the forecast path beside the path taken, the verdict, seconds and whether it was banked; the seams, runs a signal ended, prove-once violations and the summary), read back by heart2 --pushes.
      ↪ path · append · read · table
      ⚠ One file per MACHINE (heart2_pushes.jsonl in git's common dir), shared by the main checkout and every worktree - never in a work tree, so never in git status, a sandbox or a commit of this PUBLIC repo; HEART2_PUSHES_PATH is a law's seam. It writes only a file named like NAME, and a record it cannot write is said, never raised: a record costs a log line, never a push. It keeps its own copy of the common-dir walk (heart2's _git_common_dir is the fuller one). A row's v is the verdict from BEFORE the gate's closing clean run - read b (banked) beside it.
- **reel_router_wilson.py** — A7 ROUTE lock: seven sabotages proving reel_router will not let a reel's retention TAG decide its read-fate; banks into self_arming.
      ↪ prove · bank_into_proof_queue · main
      ⚠ Every attempt is SYNTHETIC — it reads no shelf, starts no sweep, spends no token and deletes no byte; the prune stays off. ONE ROW IS ONE ATTACK and `n` is how many times that attack was applied, so it banks attacks=1 with n/k separately (REG-598) — banking n as attacks would be repetition wearing confluence's clothes. _caught() works by AST walk over reel_router._string_keys_read_by, so a rename in reel_router silently makes it return False.
- **review_lite.py** — Deterministic pre-push review of the defect classes this repo was bitten by: unisolated _*_PATH state files, readers missing their throttle guards.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ add · read
      ⚠ It has no main() and no importable API — the checks run AT IMPORT TIME at module level and the module ends in sys.exit(1) on any BLOCK, so `import review_lite` terminates the caller. It is the deterministic half by design: the model pass is opt-in via TVD_REVIEW=1, PRINTS its findings and never blocks, because a reviewer that blocks on taste gets disabled within a week.
- **robot_smoke.py** — CI gate that boots the frozen TV_ROBOT multi-worker lane for ~20s in stub mode and asserts banner, journal rows and no traceback.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ main
      ⚠ The robot lane is FROZEN and an explicit arc non-goal — this gate does not mean anyone should use it; it exists only because the robot shares journal/bridge/capture code that keeps evolving and would otherwise rot silently. It spawns a real subprocess on port 17963 with PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 (block-buffered stdout would die unflushed at SIGTERM), so a concurrent run on that port collides.
- **route_wilson.py** — Deletes or renames what each route lane claims to have found in a safe_copy tree, re-runs the module in a subprocess, and banks real refusals into self_arming; zero-match attempts are dropped, not counted.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ run(say=print) · python3 tv/route_wilson.py --bank
- **rulings.py** — Reports the STANDING RULINGS that must stop a fix before it is written - a marked, auditable list of decisions Konyo has already made, with a denominator that admits what it cannot see.
      ↪ scan · main
      ⚠ ⚠ EXTRACTION WAS TRIED FIRST AND MEASURED TO FAIL: four designs, one acceptance test (find v1631's tab colour, v2397's removed hover wall, and the lane-count strip ruling). Marker+prohibition found 0 of 3; bare prohibition language returned 13,974 hits. The marker is not a reliable key, which is why the list is MARKED BY HAND rather than mined. Unindexed since v3294 (5ac970a9) and that is exactly when test_the_blueprint_names_the_engine went red on CI.
- **run_gates.py** — The gate set in one file, one exit-code verdict: every Gate row carries its law's WHY - the set-piece qlvl provenance among them - plus skip_ok and the no-orphan-suite rule.
      ↪ Gate(name, cmd, timeout, why=, skip_ok=) · test_the_set_pieces_carry_a_real_qlvl · test_the_missing_wall_shows_the_qlvl
      ⚠ A SKIP must be DECLARED IN ADVANCE (skip_ok=) or it counts with the failures — 'loud' was not enough, a gate that skips on every venue has never run at all while the run still exited 0.
- **rung_accounting_wilson.py** — Wilson evidence for the `reel.route` lock — 27 distinct attacks on one_funnel's rung accounting, which reported UNCACHED rungs as traceless.
      ↪ run · live · main
      ⚠ It attacks the REPORTING half (one_funnel), not reel_story's placement — those are two different modules answering 'where is this reel'. The v2725 defect it exists over: one_funnel printed 'no store records this rung, so passing it leaves no trace' for four of six rungs, when reel_retention.plan() answers for all 40 reels and reel_story gives all 40 a known stage. The rungs were UNCACHED and it reported that as TRACELESS. Seventeen attacks were added purely to clear HARD_BAR 0.900 (wilson_lower(N,N) needs 35 distinct attacks), and NINE went red on arrival — an attack that cannot fail inflates n and proves nothing, which is why `wilsonByAttack` exists at all.
- **sabotage_extract_gap_holding.py** — Drives test_extract_gap_holding.py RED with 8 targeted edits to extract_gap.py, counting each anchor TWICE — once with comments/docstrings blanked — so no law can be satisfied by prose.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ main · run_gate · strip_comments
      ⚠ NOTHING RUNS IT — run_gates.py cites '8 of 8 sabotages RED' in a `why=` string only; there is no Gate entry, so that claim is prose and nothing re-proves it. Both anchor counts (comment-stripped AND raw) must be exactly 1 or it REFUSES to report a result, because a sabotage that did not apply makes the law UNPROVEN, not fine. Restores by SHA-256-verified copy from a pristine snapshot, never by re-editing text — `git checkout <file>` is banned here while a fleet is running.
- **self_arming.py** — The lock ledger: banks sabotages ATTEMPTED vs guard refusals per surface, scores them with Wilson + confluence, and answers may(lock) in his required order.
      ↪ may · record · bank · score · report
      ⚠ k and n count SABOTAGES, never agreements — a lock fed by an agreement rate opens because nothing was ever tested, which is the exact failure it exists to prevent. `n == 0` is UNPROVEN, not failing. `may()` checks the ORDER first and reports the blocked upstream prerequisite rather than its own score, so a high number never reads as 'nearly there'. And the heart gate is split by REVERSIBILITY: a STALE census softens only for non-destructive surfaces, a BLIND instrument never softens. Note control_app counts OPEN as not-open while `report()` counts OPEN + HARDENED together — two tallies of one quantity.
- **shadow_ledger.py** — Sole writer of shadow_ledger.json — accumulates where the Wilson shadow rule and the live gate agree or differ on chronicle names, naming each disagreement.
      ↪ observe · state
      ⚠ It exists only because chronicle_retro's LAW 1 forbids writing — chronicle_retro SCORES, this PERSISTS, the console REPORTS. It NEVER promotes the shadow rule on its own statistics, never reports a count without naming the diverging items, and never starts a fresh count over a file it could not parse. Its path goes through the fixture root (TV_HIST) — it did NOT before v2420, and 14 tests were writing his real ledger.
- **ship_audit.py** — Audits the CLAIM, not the product: working tree + a sha-stamped run_gates receipt + gh run list CI + recorded pixels/third-eye, printed as MAY CLAIM / MAY NOT CLAIM. A skip is never a pass.
      ↪ python3 tv/ship_audit.py (the audit) · --gates (run run_gates.py, write .ship_receipt.json) · --saw-pixels PATH [--surface NAME] · --third-eye PATH · audit()
      ⚠ Evidence is sha-pinned: a receipt whose sha != HEAD is refused outright ("a gate that passed against different bytes is not evidence about these"), so --gates must be re-run after every commit. CI unread is refused, never read as green.
- **source_window.py** — The one anchored source window for every law that asserts on code as text — both ends anchored to real markers, and a missing anchor RAISES instead of returning less.
      ↪ between · after · block · block_from · strip_js_comments
      ⚠ WindowError subclasses AssertionError on purpose — a law whose subject moved has FAILED, it has not crashed. A missing END anchor raises rather than falling back to 'the rest of the file', because that fallback is the fixed-window defect one level up (68 fixed windows measured in this tree, 25 inside a function with a negative assertion). And the ORDER is the law: anchor the region, strip comments over the WHOLE of it, THEN search — windowing first can sever a `/* */` so the surviving prose satisfies the assertion.
- **stamp_leak.py** — What a version stamp may not carry: a home path, an IP, a hostname, a token or this machine's identity (leak_problem), and anything but inert words (free_text_problem). bump_version refuses a leaking or non-inert name or note with them, and gate_ledger checks every added stamp line with it before a coherent bump carries a gate.
      ↪ leak_problem · free_text_problem
      ⚠ One definition, two callers. A check that cannot load counts as dirty, never clean.
- **store_door_harness.py** — REG-2410..2417 - the store door's node stand-in: the forked-name sets and window.LSR (its quarantine, hold and release), the shape-aware boot readers and the store notice, ⟦TAB SYNC⟧, the vault module's head and its filing door, cut out of bible.html and run in ONE node process per law - for the eight laws that hold a store that will not read as named, kept aside and never written over, and another tab's write or removal as merged, never clobbered.
      ↪ page · src · between · fn · window_fn · stage · store_read · store_notice · tab_sync · vault_head · vault_door · say_line · vault_page · boot · loaders · NODE · PRELUDE
      ⚠ Every piece is CUT from bible.html by its own anchors (an anchor that moved raises, never skips), so a law asks the shipped code. page() seeds a localStorage stand-in (RAW) and runs the real window.LSR door, so a quarantine really copies bytes aside (aside() lists them) and fire() delivers a storage event the way the browser does. NODE is shutil.which('node'): an absent node skips the laws (UNMEASURED); a node that runs and fails is an AssertionError, never a skip. vault_page's door is the whole ⟦#246 VAULTFILE⟧ block with VAULT_DOOR_STUBS standing in for the router, the lane locks and the paints.
- **store_owners.py** — Declares ONE owner plus reasoned readers for each reel store and fails any undeclared module that touches one — coupling evidence, not a write proof.
      ↪ STORES · audit · main
      ⚠ It does NOT prove single-writer, and that is stated first because it is what a reader assumes. Two attempts to measure writes — a filename-adjacency grep and an AST walk resolving constants — BOTH returned zero writers for all four stores, which measured the instruments rather than the code (paths are bound in helpers and threaded through arguments). What it checks is a ratchet: every store names an owner whose source actually mentions it, every other toucher is a declared reader WITH A REASON, and a new toucher fails. Consumers read the `STORES` dict directly; there is no `report()` function despite one being referenced by name in write_census.py's comments.
- **swallow_census.py** — AST census of swallowed exceptions ranked by whether the handler hands a caller a FALSY DEFAULT after a failed read — and a ratchet gate that fails only when the RANK-1 count grows.
      ↪ scan · check · write_baseline
      ⚠ A RANK IS A PLACE IN A QUEUE, NOT A VERDICT — it cannot tell you a site is wrong, and reporting a rank as a defect would make it the very thing it was written to stop. Most of the 537 are CORRECT (`try: tab.close() except: pass` should swallow). Two refusal paths matter: a MISSING baseline prints 🔴 and returns 1 ('unconfigured is not clean'), and since v2389 a baseline whose counts.rank1 is not an int is MALFORMED, not a count of zero — that bug once reported a healthy tree as '74 NEW sites'.
- **sweep_wilson.py** — Proves the PAID sweep door refuses: chronicle_sweep_start must reject busy, no-lane, a limit of -5/0/'x', and a non-existent hist dir. No attempt has a success path. Banks under vault.sweep_start.
      ↪ score() · bank_into_proof_queue() · CLAIMS · python3 tv/sweep_wilson.py
      ⚠ It shares the 'A2 · step 1' label with printer_wilson but guards a DIFFERENT lock — vault.sweep_start (bar 0.510, the door that spends his money) versus printer.stream. It swaps _CHRON_JOB and _chron_lanes only inside a finally, and `limit=None` is deliberately untested because None legitimately means ALL.
- **tasks_freshness.py** — Gate: a TASKS.md READY row whose fingerprint string has vanished is finished work still listed as ready; unfingerprinted rows are named UNKNOWN.
      ↪ check · prove · main
      ⚠ It never edits TASKS.md — editing fixes today and drifts again next ship, which is why the table sat 34 versions stale. The fingerprint (`<!--fp: bible.html :: fv.onclick-->`) is the string whose PRESENCE means the work is undone, so the gate inverts the usual sense. Fingerprints are anchored and COUNTED, not grepped, because a correction note describing a fixed bug satisfies a naive search for that bug. Only the undone/READY tables are graded; shipped tables are history. `--prove` makes it go red for its own reason.
- **vault_page_node.py** — V1c (#152 item 4): the WHOLE bible.html in node - every inline <script> in page order in one global world over a small DOM/localStorage stand-in (ids from the markup and from every innerHTML it writes), storage starting EMPTY, timers queued until the body flushes them, and no network at all (fetch, XHR, WebSocket, EventSource, sendBeacon inert). run(body) boots the page, fires DOMContentLoaded and load, runs the body and returns the OUT it filled; PATCHES are exact find/replace pairs applied to the source first (a law counts calls with them), a PRELUDE runs before the first page script, and clock/seed make two runs comparable byte for byte. The vault laws that must see a hand add as it ships (test_a_hand_add_is_not_quadratic, test_the_vault_memo_changes_no_answer) and the speed bench stand on it.
      ↪ run · program · NODE · BIBLE · HARNESS_JS
      ⚠ It is not a browser: querySelectorAll answers [], layout is zero, and innerHTML costs nothing, so it measures the page's JavaScript and never its DOM (a Chrome-only cost needs a Playwright spec on CI). A PATCH whose anchor matches the wrong number of times fails the run by name (PATCH ANCHOR MOVED), never silently. The page's console goes to stderr and OUT travels on one marked stdout line, because a page log after the body would otherwise be read as the answer. A script that throws at boot is listed in OUT.__boot.errors - a law must assert that list is empty, or it is measuring half a page.
- **verdict_provenance.py** — Census over the console's stores asking whether each stored verdict names WHAT produced it: ANSWERS/PARTIAL/SILENT/REFERENCE/UNKNOWN.
      ↪ census · ratchet · write_baseline · main
      ⚠ The census figures are RESTATED in eight places and five of them went stale (41/4/3/21/12/1 in prose against the tool's own 44/6/4/16/17/1), so every restatement must carry the DATE it was measured. REFERENCE (a clockless roster or lookup table) is NOT a missing stamp — the first cut had no REFERENCE class and cried '18 of 21 missing' with 24 unreadable subjects, measuring itself. ratchet() is red only if the gap GREW, and it returns 1 (UNKNOWN, not clean) when the baseline is absent, unparseable, or predates trackedNames.
- **version_stamp_gate.py** — Reads the last commit SUBJECT: if it names vNNNN, the four stamps (D2R_BUILD.id, tv_diablo.VERSION, WINDOWS_SHIP.json, control_app ver) must equal the HIGHEST named. Writes nothing; silent when none.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ python3 tv/version_stamp_gate.py · main() · subject() · leading_versions() · STAMPS
      ⚠ STAMPS must learn any fifth surface bump_version learns — its first draft listed only three and would have passed a tree whose control_app stamp was left behind. It reads only the LEADING version cluster, so `revert what v1900 broke` is not judged, and a subject naming no version passes in silence.
- **worker_source.py** — Lifts the beacon worker's shared top-level helpers out of functions/api/console.js for laws that run one shaper of it in node.
      ↪ prelude
      ⚠ Nothing in the console imports it - only node laws do, to put the beacon worker's SHIPPED top-level helpers (cutAtWord, functions/api/console.js) in front of a block they lift and run alone. Each helper is cut from the worker by its own declaration and must occur exactly once (assert); a copy written here would be what the law grades instead of the worker (REG-1839).
- **write_census.py** — Arms write_witness over a REAL write to a scratch root and reads back WHO wrote each reel store — observation, not coupling declaration.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ census · main
      ⚠ Nothing imports it — run_gates.py:3399 registers it as Gate('write_census'). A store nobody exercised reports NOT EXERCISED WITH A REASON, never '0 writers' (the tombstone needs a real deletion and the prune stays OFF; vault stores need a PAID sweep). Every exercise runs against a scratch root, never his stores. The only witnessed event comes from the `os.replace` patch, because these stores are written as <name>.tmp then moved — three earlier instruments each returned a zero that measured themselves, and it once shipped a false green by calling a `store_owners.report()` that does not exist.

### guest (2)

- **guest_fixture_pack.py** — Turns one recorded reel into a versioned, checksummed regression pack the guest seat can click: a representative subset of frames, flagged as a fixture, loadable only into a mirror.
      ↪ build · verify · load
      ⚠ A pack is NOT a copied reel: one reel measured 196 MB / 153 frames, a pack of it is 2.7 MB / 16, spaced EVENLY so the scrub bar still travels the whole run (a head slice leaves the reel controls untestable past the opening). ⚠ A fixture gets its OWN namespaced sessionId — keeping the source's made the loader dedupe it against the real session list and the real row won, so the fixture flag never reached the guest. load() refuses any destination under tv/frames: a pack may be BUILT from footage and never LOADED into it.
- **guest_profile.py** — The Grok actor for the guest seat plus the redaction that keeps a public mirror publishable: a stable identity, and a deep scrub of the owner's install id, hostname, user and home paths out of every payload the box will serve.
      ↪ profile · scrub · rewrite_status · unreadable · is_unreadable · leaks · progress_seed
      ⚠ The rewrite is a REDACTION, not a relabel. The guest seat renders a mirror of the live console and this repo is public, so scrub() walks dict KEYS as well as values — per-session dumps are KEYED by path, and a values-only walk leaks the home directory one filename at a time. The identity is REPLACED rather than merged so an unknown key cannot ride along. ⚠ leaks() and the scrub read the SAME pair list, so a detector that forgets a secret is blind to it — callers must also check literals.

### heart-organs (36)

- **audit_child.py** — #172 phase B: runs a cold source audit (the route census, the lane spans of control_app.py) in a short-lived child that writes the same cache the console reads, so ~600 MB of parse memory leaves with the child.
      ↪ wanted · run · hit · status
      ⚠ Only a CONSOLE spawns (frame_ref.on_console_path(), set by control_app.main()); every law, the gate and CI derive in their own process, because a child cannot see a monkeypatch. A child carries TVD_AUDIT_CHILD=1 so it never spawns a child. It FAILS OPEN: a child that times out or exits non-zero leaves the console to derive itself, and STATE says so.
- **child_guard.py** — #83 (REG-1515) the child supervisor: one spawn door per role (capture, vision readers, the stall reader, OCR) that ends the previous instance's whole tree before starting the next, a per-parent ledger so a restarted console ends its dead predecessor's children, a rescue-loop watchdog for unrecorded processes of known roles, a census by family for the doctor's one_of_each row, and the memory floor under which no secondary worker starts.
      ↪ spawn · end · census · watchdog_tick · free_ram_mb · secondary_spawn_allowed · status
      ⚠ spawn(role, argv) ENDS the role's previous tree first (verified by pid + creation time; a reused pid is dropped, never killed) and waits a bounded END_WAIT_S - a role holds one process, ever. Windows children go into a Job Object with KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE (ctypes), POSIX children lead their own session (killpg); the Mac AGENT must not come through it (v779, the Screen Recording TCC chain). The birth reader never spawns a subprocess (sysctl / /proc / GetProcessTimes): a `ps` here was counted as a capture by every console law that stubs subprocess.Popen. UNKNOWN free RAM never refuses a spawn. REG-1550: a LIVE parent's child is never the watchdog's to end (ours -> bypass, said; a stranger's -> counted; measured on his Mac, the first cut named the console's own closer OCR worker and warm reader TO_END); a kill needs a table row carrying this checkout's path and a known role, a birth match through ONE reader (the table's lstart/CIM clock is re-read through sysctl/GetProcessTimes - the DST hour disagrees by 3600 s), and the birth re-asked at the kill; an UNKNOWN table, parent birth or pid birth ends nothing. REG-1551: ceilings are per OWNER except the capture's (checkout-wide); `owed` is the survivors still alive; a post-execv console adopts its own previous life's records (same pid, same birth) before its first ledger write.
- **chronicle_doctor.py** — Doctor for the chronicle arc — OK/MISSING/UNKNOWN per check on THIS machine: Grok login, footage, grouping, board apply. Never fixes.
      ↪ diagnose
      ⚠ UNKNOWN is not a failure and must never be rendered as one. A check that raises is caught and returned as UNKNOWN ('the check itself failed'), never as MISSING — 'I could not check' and 'it is broken' are different sentences.
- **chronicle_routes.py** — Derives each chronicle route's lanes from the *_roster.json artifacts on disk and grades them FLOWING/WATCHED/DARK/UNKNOWN in the heart's vocabulary.
      ↪ routes · corroborate · cache_reset · tally_memo_key
      ⚠ It carries NO table of routes — routes are discovered from disk, and its corroborator only BADGES a divergent sibling, it enforces nothing. routes() is memoised twice (in-process _MEMO plus .chronicle_routes_cache.json) because a cold derive cost 16.1s, so a stale cache is what you read unless cache_reset() runs.
- **code_identity.py** — Answers whether THIS interpreter's loaded bytecode agrees with the source on disk, by comparing each function's co_firstlineno against where the file says it starts. Built after three pushes of one identical tree read green, RED, green, with the red one's traceback naming a line and a variable that do not exist in the file. Distinct from console_doctor's 'running code matches disk', which asks the CONSOLE over the wire about its own image.
      ↪ drift · report · say
      ⚠ It CANNOT be built from mtime and size - that is exactly what CPython uses to decide a .pyc is fresh, so a check from the same two inputs agrees with it by construction, collisions included. The only witness that survives one is co_firstlineno, which comes from the BYTECODE while the source is read fresh. Three instrument bugs on the way, each caught by a count: functools.wraps copies __module__ onto a wrapper whose code lives in contextlib (two unrelated modules both reported drifted at the SAME line 242), CPython points co_firstlineno at the FIRST DECORATOR so accepting any '@' line swallowed everything, and skipping COMMENT lines while walking to the def let a function that had moved 40 lines be re-found and reported clean. Decorators and blanks only. And on this Mac the cache is NOT in __pycache__ - sys.pycache_prefix puts it under ~/Library/Caches/com.apple.python.
- **console_doctor.py** — The EAGLE EYE: one pass over the RUNNING console; each check answers OK / MISSING(action) / UNKNOWN(why), never a bare boolean.
      ↪ run · report · slow_surface · tick_caches · browser_suites_among
      ⚠ report() does NOT run the checks — every row comes back UNMEASURED, because the organ interface is asked about COVERAGE, not findings; only run()/report(deep=True) executes, and it reaches into his live window via /api/board_ownership (JS evaluated in the window he is looking at) plus ~2 min of SLOW checks. It REPORTS: --fix touches only the provably reversible, and it calls the other doctors rather than re-implementing their checks.
- **console_healer.py** — Scar ledger plus three re-derivable remedies: fold orphan frames into the reel their own stamp names, restore a vault store from a healthy backup, clear a stale theatre-fault. TV_AUTO_HEAL arms it.
      ↪ record() · heal() · tend() · history() · says()
      ⚠ Silent means OFF — without TV_AUTO_HEAL=1 it still scars everything and only reports what it WOULD have done. A heal that leaves the check red disarms that remedy for the life of the process; returning "nothing to do" is NOT a failure (v2080 retired a working remedy that way).
- **corroborate.py** — THE CORROBORATOR - cross-engine invariants asserting the engines AGREE (the frame deleter never looser than the reel planner, one `owned` against another); it never writes and never averages.
      ↪ run() / selftest() · _inv_the_deleter_is_never_looser_than_the_planner · _inv_the_two_deleters_stay_at_their_own_granularity · _inv_the_deleter_releases_only_what_the_store_declares · _inv_the_two_owned_fields
      ⚠ Each invariant carries `prove` and selftest() drives every one to RED against synthetic inputs — a corroborator nobody has seen disagree is exactly the green that lies; UNKNOWN is reported as UNKNOWN, never as agreement.
- **dead_field.py** — Reports fields present on every row of a watched store and filled on none — a dead field — as evidence on each heart read.
      ↪ state · dead_fields · main
      ⚠ It REFUSES NOTHING — no build fails, no button blocks; it is evidence for a reader. And below MIN_ROWS=30 a column of nulls is UNKNOWN, not clean, because a zero over rows that could not disagree measures the sample. The `WATCHED` entries are 3- OR 4-tuples (the optional 4th is a declared-null map), so indexing blindly at [3] breaks the older rows. Born from `reel_retention._tombstone` writing None 410/410 times.
- **exec_hygiene.py** — Stops the warm workers an os.execv relaunch would orphan (module globals holding a live Popen in .p with .stop()), and at boot reaps the children inherited from the replaced image BY PID. Called by control_app._before_exec at every exec site and first thing in main().
      ↪ quiesce_before_exec · children_of · reap_inherited · warm_workers
      ⚠ Never a blanket waitpid(-1): it would steal the exit status of the console's own Popen children, and CPython reads ECHILD as returncode 0 — a failed child would report success. children_of() returns None (UNKNOWN) when ps cannot be read, never [].
- **fleet_routes.py** — Judges each fleet-card lane (getter→probe→total→unit) for sets/uniques/runewords as FLOWING/WATCHED/DARK/UNKNOWN, quoting route_totals and chronicle_routes.corroborate rather than its own copy.
      ↪ routes(tally=None)
- **health_engine.py** — The console's one red/green flag engine: nine checks (lanes, armed_migration, board_join, orphans, shelf witnesses, shadow_watch, readers_agree, self_arming, lane_liveness) each returning ok/warn/blocked/unknown.
      ↪ report · say · check_armed_migrations · check_board_join · main
      ⚠ IT REPORTS AND NEVER REPAIRS (a test pins that nothing here writes, deletes or restarts) — and it is not an AUTHORITY either: frame_authority owns deletion, chronicle_retro owns grounding, this only reports on them. UNKNOWN is a first-class state and never renders as ok; a check that raises becomes UNKNOWN, not a failure.
- **heart.py** — Derives every self-running vessel into ONE vocabulary — FLOWING / WATCHED / DARK / UNKNOWN — from lane_census, health rows and the lock ledger.
      ↪ vessels · main
      ⚠ The FLOWING branch is documented as UNREACHABLE with real data: `scored` is keyed on the ORGAN rows' ids (lanes, readers, selfArming, board_join) while `watcher` is a LANE name (tvd-eagle-watch and siblings) — two disjoint vocabularies, so `scored.get(watcher)` is always None. So a 'WATCHED, go sabotage it' prompt names work that cannot land. Also: it NEVER writes or caches (a gate asserts the module opens no file), counts are derived not typed, DARK != UNKNOWN, and WATCHED is work owed, not a fault.
- **heart2.py** — HEART 2.0 — the census of the gates themselves: re-tampers every guard that declares a RED_PROOF in a sandbox to prove it can still go red, and classifies each as proven / blind / unproven / unprovable.
      ↪ report · prove · detect · triage · gates_fingerprint
      ⚠ v1 asks whether the SYSTEM is healthy; v2 asks whether the INSTRUMENTS can still go red — measured 2026-09-08, the heart was green while 12 of 238 gates were red and 8 were blind. Tampering happens ONLY in a safe_copy.py sandbox (never cp -R: tv/ is 5,865 MB of footage and a full copy once caused ENOSPC), and it PROPOSES into .heart2_proposals.md — it never edits a guard. A gate that fails its CLEAN run is UNPROVABLE, not BLIND; a sabotage whose `find` matches ≠ `matches` is the usual culprit, so the match count is printed. It is a RATCHET (the unproven backlog may only shrink), not a ban.
- **heart2_candidates.py** — Derives a ready-to-run red-proof (sabotage) candidate from a gate's own assertIn literals, so the proposal is actionable instead of a name.
      ↪ candidates_for · render_block · infrastructure · target_files
      ⚠ It refuses far more often than it proposes — anchor shorter than 8 chars, zero matches, more than one match, or an unresolvable target file all abort with a named reason. It returns TEXT only: it never writes to a guard and never writes to a target file.
- **heart_map.py** — Derives HEART.md from the tree: every `id` the console PAINTS (the honest unit — a class may be shared by forty nodes, an id names one thing), and which of them any watcher mentions. UNWATCHED is not a defect; what is refused is a surface going unwatched SILENTLY, so the count is a ratchet that may not fall without a reason in heart_floor.json.
      ↪ surfaces · watched · measure · render · main
      ⚠ `--check` REFUSES and never regenerates, exactly like blueprint.py, because the pre-push grades the WORKING TREE. ⚠ And an unreadable input is a REFUSAL, never a zero: `_read` returns None (v3231 — it used to return "", so an unreadable control_ui.html gave an empty id set and would have BANKED a HEART.md claiming the console paints 0 surfaces). A watcher that cannot be read is named rather than skipped, because dropping it makes every surface it covers look UNWATCHED — a coverage collapse indistinguishable from a real one. `_why_unmeasurable` is TOLD what the caller found and must never re-read the file: bump_version replaces the surfaces atomically and regenerates this map in the same run, so a second look can see a healthy file and strip the reason out of the refusal (v3235).
- **input_latency.py** — Holds the click-to-paint beat the page reports ({p50, p95, maxLongtask, n}) for health_engine and the console, plus the v3769 latency baseline and the v3773 count budget.
      ↪ record · read
      ⚠ A missing beat is None (UNKNOWN), never 0 or instant. P95_BUDGET_MS and LONGTASK_BUDGET_MS are wall-clock and move with box load, so the v3773 guard (test_input_stays_responsive) does not judge on them; it judges COUNT_BUDGET, the getComputedStyle calls and whole-document walks per tab switch and per vault repaint.
- **lane_census.py** — Classifies every threading.Thread target in control_app.py as LOOP / one-shot / UNKNOWN, and diffs that census against BLUEPRINT.md's LANES section.
      ↪ census · classify · blueprint_disagreement · prove · supervisor_set_is_current
      ⚠ NOT every thread is a lane — a one-shot worker is a TASK and registering it would promise persistence it does not have, so 'started but not registered' is not automatically 'unwatched'. It carries its own `--prove` because an earlier ad-hoc version misclassified five `while True` `_loop` functions as one-shot and that answer was quoted into a GitHub issue.
- **lane_health.py** — Per extraction lane: freshness (when it last produced work), reach (corpus covered), and divergence (two lanes disagreeing about the same session).
      ↪ lane · divergence · report · owed_counts · say
      ⚠ The stall thresholds are an explicitly LABELLED judgement, tuned against nothing. It decides nothing and writes nothing, and an unreadable store answers UNKNOWN rather than healthy — it exists because a watchdog that spoke only on message-change made five days of silence read as fine.
- **lane_liveness.py** — THREAD-ALIVE for console loops: each loop ticks with its OWN period; rows grade FLOWING/LATE/UNTIMED/UNKNOWN/DORMANT.
