      ↪ tick · dormant · waking · rows · stamping_functions
      ⚠ _TICKS is PROCESS memory — a cold import outside control_app reports all 20 vessels UNKNOWN, which is a fact about the reader, not the lanes; use stamping_functions(), which parses the SOURCE and answers from anywhere. The period travels WITH the stamp (a global threshold spans 0.5s to 900s, 1800x), and every_s vs dead_after_s are two different questions — padding every_s makes the report print a period the lane does not have. Ages are monotonic so a sleeping Mac is not eight hours of silence.
- **lane_ports.py** — Gives each heart2 prove lane its own port base so two sandboxes do not share 17971 or 17972, which is how one sandbox request deletes a tracked file in the other.
      ↪ base_for_lane · current_base · adopt · adopt_agent · adopt_control · stamp
      ⚠ It never binds a socket and never deletes a file. A forbidden or out-of-range base is refused and current_base returns None, so adopt keeps the suite historical port. keep=True preserves a port the caller already set only when no lane base is present; a set TV_LANE_PORT_BASE overwrites it. stamp also sets TV_LAW_PORT to base plus 2 so two lanes do not share a Chrome port. His console port is in the forbidden set and is never derived.
- **lane_trace.py** — Writes one small per-lane file recording WHAT A LOOP DECIDED (drift verdict, orphan count, shadow result) so an outside process can date the loop — the second, independent witness a corroborator needs.
      ↪ note · read · age_ms · note_dormant · path_of
      ⚠ A TICK IS NOT A SECOND WITNESS — lane_liveness._TICKS is an in-process dict on a monotonic clock and reads EMPTY from another process. A bare heartbeat would be an ECHO of the watchdog, so this must carry the RESULT: a loop running and producing nothing then reads as a CONTRADICTION, which is the only thing this organ can see. Writes are atomic (tmp + os.replace) because a plain open(p,'w') truncates first and an empty file reads as 'the loop produced nothing'. With min_gap_s the TRACE period is the GAP, not the tick period, and loop_corroborate.LOOPS must declare the gap or a correctly-throttled loop reads stale. note() swallows everything and returns False.
- **loop_corroborate.py** — Corroborates a background loop from two independent witnesses: its in-process TICK and the dated TRACE it leaves. Fresh tick, stale trace = the finding.
      ↪ corroborate · report · main
      ⚠ Six of eight loops leave no datable trace and are honestly ABSENT rather than covered — inventing an organ for them would remanufacture the fake coverage v3055 deleted. And lane_liveness._TICKS is an in-process dict on a monotonic clock, so a separate process reads ZERO lanes on a healthy machine: a missing tick is UNKNOWN, never dead.
- **organ_matrix.py** — Measures which surfaces each of the four organs (eagle / watchdog / doctor / corroborator) actually NAMES: COVERED, ABSENT or UNKNOWN.
      ↪ matrix · organ_coverage · surfaces · comparability · main
      ⚠ It does NOT fill the table in — a matrix that reported coverage it cannot demonstrate is worse than the empty one he was shown. UNKNOWN (the organ has no callable report / would not import) is not ABSENT, and two of the four organs are UNKNOWN everywhere on this tree. The synonym list in `_names_from` is load-bearing and fails SILENTLY when short: missing `key` made a live corroborator read as dead, and missing `check` would have flipped console_doctor from an honest 'cannot be asked' to 44 confident ABSENT cells with no error.
- **page_delta.py** — #160 (REG-1753) - the pre-push hook and Routine I ask whether bible.html changed beyond its build stamp (window.D2R_BUILD, rewritten by every bump) before re-grading the page; a stamp-only change skips render, the Playwright smoke, and the 21-minute suite.
      ↪ stamp_only · page_changed · routine_i_decision · main
      ⚠ FAILS CLOSED: anything it cannot read (a bad range, git failing, undecodable bytes, a new branch, a forced push) answers RUN so the gates run. It pins bump_version's WHOLE stamp line, so code appended to the stamp is a page change; if bump_version's format moves, every bump reads as a page change (the law test_a_version_stamp_is_not_a_page_change goes red on the tree's own stamp). The render and smoke triggers ask page_changed. Routine I asks routine_i_decision, which also runs the suite when a spec, the Playwright config, the console page, a package file, or the workflow changed. Deploy still keys on the file name.
- **proof_carrier.py** — #186 step 3 (REG-2737, his ruling) - carries one Windows PC's proven laws to another on the same commit through /api/proofs, so Dean's PC does not re-prove what the ALT proved over byte-identical code.
      ↪ publish · take · export_payload · head_of · run_heart2 · http_get · http_post
      ⚠ It never trusts a peer's proof: take() only imports an export carried at THIS checkout's own HEAD on this platform, and heart2 --import-proved re-keys every entry on this PC's bytes and banks only what matches (REG-2630). publish() never posts unsigned - the route stores only a body signed by the key the roster already registered for that owner. The console runs it on win32 only, when he is measured NOT playing and memory is at the prover's start floor.
- **proof_keys_mac.py** — REG-2590 - the Mac driver: push HEAD to ci/proof-keys-<sha12>, prove alongside only the gates CI cannot bank, wait for this commit's fan-in, download its set and `heart2 --import-shards` it.
      ↪ main · local_gates · ci_ref · fan_in_state · download_artifact · owed_after_import
      ⚠ Pushes only refs/heads/ci/proof-keys-<sha12>. The token is read by git's credential helper from the environment and sent only to api.github.com; the artifact redirect is followed without it. An unkeyable gate is never run alongside (never banked), so the final push still runs it. After the import it says FULL or PARTIAL (REG-2594): every assigned proof CI banked nothing for and this Mac does not prove alongside is owed; a PARTIAL set exits 3 and never runs --final.
- **proof_shard.py** — REG-2585/2586/2593/2595 - one heart2 prove split across N CI runners: the cost-balanced plan (--proof-shard K/N) and the checks that bring the shards' banked PROVENs home (--import-shards), and the post-push census (--census) that names the laws the next batch fixes.
      ↪ plan · measure_costs · proof_costs · check_shards · select · merge_into · fan_in · census
      ⚠ A plan is a pure function of the gate names, their proofs, tv/gate_costs.json and tv/proof_costs.json (REG-2593: seconds per proof MEASURED on a runner; refresh with --measure-costs DIR) at one commit: every shard must compute it alike, so the import refuses shards whose plan digests differ. The import is ALL OR NOTHING and re-derives every key from this checkout's bytes; it never rewrites `tree`, so #242's clean wave still runs each gate clean here. A shard that kept going (REG-2591) lists its failures as `owed`; the fan-in names them and still passes a whole set.
- **reap_shape.py** — Decides from the AST whether a function reaps its first parameter (wait/poll/Thread(target=p.wait)) on EVERY path it can take, and whether a call hands a Popen to such a helper. Used by test_control's test_no_ASSIGNED_popen_goes_unreaped; driven by test_a_conditional_reap_is_not_a_reaper.
      ↪ reaps_first_param · passes_to
      ⚠ A reap counts only at the function's own level, as the FIRST statement of a try body, in a finally, or in a with body — never inside an if/loop/except and never behind a return/raise. 'First in the try' is the v3421 lesson: a BrokenPipeError on stdin.close() earlier in the same try skipped the reap for three weeks. Nested defs are not the function's own path.
- **self_prove.py** — #50 (REG-1447) - every PC proves its own instruments: when this machine's heart census is absent or stale for the gates on disk, start a background prove so self_arming's locks can open on the ALT and Dean's PC without anyone pushing from them.
      ↪ tick · decide · census_state · tree_state · spawn · ensure_prover_deps
      ⚠ Runs ONLY on an installed console (clean tree at its upstream) - on a development tree the pre-push gate is the prover and a background prove would fight it for the CPU. It never decides a verdict: it starts `heart2.py --prove` hidden (BELOW_NORMAL + CREATE_NO_WINDOW on Windows, nice 15 elsewhere, HEART2_DEADLINE_SCALE=4) only while the machine is idle, and heart2 writes the census that self_arming reads. The Mac's census does not speak for another PC: measured 2026-09-29 the ALT's gate fingerprint differed at the same commit and one law was BLIND there while red on the Mac.
- **shard_suite.py** — #42 lever 4 (REG-1740) - the push gate runs test_control as N parallel shards by class (default 4, SUITE_SHARDS), dealt largest-first by last measured cost; 435.6 s serial became 66-88 s.
      ↪ classes_of · deal · run · main
      ⚠ Green ONLY when every shard exited 0, ran exactly the cases it was dealt, and every class the loader found was dealt - a shard that dies before answering or runs less is RED. Each shard runs its classes in the serial run's (alphabetical) order and gets its own TV_PORT/TV_CONTROL_PORT; a TERM ends the shards. The per-class cost file tv/.suite_class_cost.json is a gitignored per-machine hint, never a verdict. CI still runs test_control whole and serial.
- **suite_verdict.py** — #42 lever 2 (REG-1720) - the push stops re-running test_agent / test_control over a commit that already passed them: `--run` stamps a green run to the commit's tree, the pre-push hook `--check`s before each heavy suite and `--record`s its own green run.
      ↪ tree_key · reusable · record · check · run · main
      ⚠ A GREEN python-suite run is reused at push time ONLY on the commit's exact git tree (every tracked file unchanged, its own records aside), the same python and platform, and younger than 6 h; a red run is never stored, a run of the same bytes still in flight is waited for. The store lives in git's COMMON dir - one per machine, shared by main and every worktree. Untracked files are outside the key (stated, as heart2's proof cache states); CI still runs both suites in full. SUITE_VERDICT_REUSE=0 closes it.
- **tooltip_oracle_lane.py** — #41 rank 16 (REG-1560): the runtime joint for the character builder's tooltip. Runs the SHIPPED composition (node over the bible.html on disk, through cb_node_harness) over their planner's measured tooltip oracle (203 rows over 6 runewords) on the console's own cadence (the tvd-tooltip-oracle lane) and writes a PER-ROW receipt - {runeword, base, agree, why} per row, the bible.html stamp it measured, the oracle's limit, lifetime counters. verdict() feeds the doctor row 'their tooltip rows', contract() speaks the shared lane vocabulary, and the corroborate joint 'their-tooltip-rows-are-ours' compares the receipt against the oracle file.
      ↪ measure · judge · compose · oracle_rows · oracle_limit · run_once · verdict · contract · read_receipt · write_receipt · freshness · bible_stamp · HELP · main
      ⚠ UNKNOWN is never 0 or OK: no receipt, node absent (a PC without node), a receipt whose bible.html sha1 is not the file on disk (stale-reading), a summary in place of rows, or an oracle it cannot read now all read UNKNOWN; a row never judged or not theirs reads MISSING and is NAMED. The gate test_the_tooltip_is_the_games_tooltip drives the SAME measure(), so the gate and the lane share one composition run and one judge. The limit is stated in every verdict: six runewords, one class, one level - a green vouches for those rows and nothing beyond them. It writes only its own receipt (tmp + os.replace) in the lane's world; an unreadable previous receipt carries the counters as None with countersWhy, never restarted at zero silently.
- **unknown_age.py** — Stamps firstUnknownTs/lastAttemptTs/unknownCount onto UNKNOWN health rows so a 5-minute and a 45-hour unknown stop rendering alike.
      ↪ attach · age_say · path
      ⚠ It STAMPS only — it never promotes UNKNOWN to ok or missing because time passed; an UNKNOWN that ages is still UNKNOWN. _load() distinguishes None (file exists and will not parse = UNKNOWN, and attach() must not _save over it) from {} (no file yet = measured-empty). path() reads TV_UNKNOWN_AGE at CALL time, so tests can redirect it.
- **vault_doctor.py** — Why the vault is empty, from his own film and free: no footage vs no stash panels vs panels with no readable names. Reports, never fixes.
      ↪ diagnose
      ⚠ Free by construction — local pixel work only, no model turn, no console, no network — so it can run while the console is down, which is exactly when someone asks. Doctrine borrowed wholesale from chronicle_doctor: OK / MISSING / UNKNOWN, and UNKNOWN is 'I could not check', never 'it is broken'. It samples (SAMPLE_EVERY=12, SAMPLE_CAP=240 frames), so 'no stash panels found' is a sampled answer, not an exhaustive one.
- **win_relaunch.py** — Makes a console relaunch and boot leave a trace on every OS (tv/.console_boot.log, tv/.relaunch_receipt.json) and, on Windows where os.execv starts a new pid, makes the child wait for its parent to exit before it takes the primary mutex and binds :17772.
      ↪ boot_log · install_excepthook · write_receipt · read_receipt · wait_for_parent
      ⚠ Never raises into the boot path. wait_for_parent pops TVD_RELAUNCH_PARENT so it cannot leak into the next relaunch; on POSIX the parent is the same pid and nothing is waited for. Windows behaviour was measured on the ALT box, not simulated.

### ledger-profile (22)

- **bake_seed.py** — Bakes bible.html's shipped _GRAIL_SEED / _SET_SEED from his LIVE board store (WebKit sqlite) so a fresh profile shows real progress; reports unless --write.
      ↪ bake · find_board_store · read_board · one_shot_owned · game_says_missing
      ⚠ The board is NOT in Chrome — Chrome holds a months-stale copy. It reads pywebview/WKWebView sqlite (UTF-16-LE values) under ~/Library/WebKit/com.apple.python3/…/LocalStorage, and the three origin dirs must be copied to SEPARATE dirs or the small ones overwrite the board and it cheerfully reports zero. Four safety laws: report-only without --write, NEVER shrink, never seed a piece _SET_MISSING says the game lacks, never seed a name a v1692/v1693 boot one-shot owns. No store found = exit 2.
- **board_identity_sweep.py** — Classifies `.board_identity.json` as DRIFTED / GUEST / OK / UNKNOWN and sweeps only the first two, backed up first.
      ↪ classify · sweep · read · console_is_running · path_for
      ⚠ The standing ritual `rm -f tv/.board_identity.json after any CDP session` is WRONG for an `ok` record: removing his live owner record degrades ok->unknown and the next write starts a fresh id with `previous: None` — the exact shape that makes his real board render as a stranger's world. It also REFUSES while his console is running (port probe), and 'no file' reports UNKNOWN, not success. No production module imports it; only its gate test and the CLI.
- **build_runeword_roster.py** — Regenerates tv/runeword_roster.json from bible.html's `const RUNEWORDS`, and reports whether that roster has gone stale against the page.
      ↪ build · is_stale · extract
      ⚠ `build()` STAMPS sourceHash, it does not CHECK it. Staleness is only detected by `is_stale()`, which nothing on the machine called until v2455 — the one roster lane of three with no watcher. Both the qualified form (`Spirit (sword)`) and the bare form are in the roster on purpose.
- **char_select.py** — #91 (REG-1581) - learns his characters from the reels' own CHARACTER-SELECT screen. A two-panel pixel test finds the screen (right list panel near-grey, left panel a dim warm grey; the crop is 0.385 x the frame HEIGHT because D2R scales its UI with height), the list crop is read by tv_diablo.charselect_read, and every read is folded into a per-PC ledger (tv/.char_roster.json, gitignored). Rides the vault autoread loop as rider lane tvd-char-learner; GET /api/chars_learned feeds the character builder, which groups level-1 characters as mules.
      ↪ tick · status · learned · owed · looks_like_char_select · panel_stats · panel_crop · normalize · record · load · save
      ⚠ A character is LEARNED only after MIN_VISITS=2 separate visits (frames >90 s apart split visits) - one misread never becomes a character. Level = the highest level two visits saw; two reads inside ONE visit keep the lower; the shown spelling is the one most reads agree on. The paid reader is capped (2 reads per visit, 8 per hour) and a tick is bounded to 8 s of scanning. load() returns None for a ledger that exists and cannot be read, and tick() then writes NOTHING - an empty ledger saved over the real one is the defect it refuses. The FIFO deletes reels; their visits stay in the ledger.
- **chronicle_crossref.py** — Answers whether reel-read item names are actually NEW or already in his ledger — 347 proposed names crossrefed to 319 already-owned, 28 new.
      ↪ crossref · canon · say
      ⚠ canon() is the COMPARISON key only, never a display name: it folds curly to straight apostrophes because 202 of his names carry a straight one and 4 a curly one and bible.html holds BOTH byte forms of the same names — raw-byte compares file one item as two and inflate 'new'. And if the ledger cannot be read, measured=False: the caller MUST render that, not a count.
- **chronicle_rebuild.py** — Derives each owned name's date and provenance from the OTHER ledger stores (foundLog/gameFound/rwMade/evidence) under DATE_AUTHORITY, and returns a proposal — a rebuild, not a snapshot replay.
      ↪ rebuild
      ⚠ It rebuilds the LEDGER STORES, never the tally — re-deriving funiScan's 292 here produced 298 because `_norm` is a different fold from `_regKey`. Also: a store the caller could not read must be passed as None, NOT {} — {} means 'empty', None means 'unknown', and rebuild() refuses to collapse them. And it WRITES NOTHING; the board presses its own door.
- **chronicle_regate.py** — Re-judges stored chronicle evidence under current rules and re-publishes it with a FRESH savedTs stamp so the board's adoption dedupe fires.
      ↪ regate · main
      ⚠ `control_app.chronicle_regate()` is a DIFFERENT function with the same name that only re-scores in memory — this module is the ONE writer (test_provenance.py:501 pins that). Its whole reason to exist is the stamp: `_chronAutoAdopt` dedupes on `proposal.startedTs`/`savedTs`, so a re-gate done by hand keeps the ORIGINAL savedTs and the board silently refuses every newly grounded name with a message that reads like success. `regate()` is pure; nothing is written without `--write`.
- **chronicle_resolve.py** — Folds raw Chronicle reader names — OCR slips (Naglring→Nagelring), base-item debris, truncations — onto unique_roster.json before witnesses are counted; unresolvable names are RETIRED with a reason.
      ↪ canonical(name, roster) · classify(name, roster, grounded) · fold_proposal(...)
- **chronicle_total.py** — Banks the dated CASC re-derivation of the Chronicle denominator — 439 rows minus 24 disableChronicle = 415, minus 12 unspawnable = 403 — and re-derives it from uniqueitems.txt.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ count
      ⚠ NOT the same file as route_totals.py, and nothing imports it — it is a hand-run record plus recipe (`--count uniqueitems.txt`), written because the OUTPUT was lost once when /tmp/casc_extract died. 403 is correct and the in-game panel reading of 404 in bible.html's own comment was a MISREAD. The game file itself is deliberately not committed (public repo, Blizzard data). 396 distinct names vs roster 398 is an OPEN measurement, not a defect.
- **counter_ledger.py** — The game's Remaining page as a NEGATIVE ledger — the only reader that can subtract: falsifies board rows, derives owned-by-subtraction.
      ↪ load · owned_by_subtraction · contradicted · denied · resolve_all
      ⚠ `load()` returns None, never an empty list — a consumer that reads 'the game was never asked' as 'the game agrees' is the exact failure this file exists to end. Every row carries `readAt`/`ageDays`, and `d2r_setPieces` is an UNDATED array of strings, so for most pieces the order of reading vs finding is UNKNOWN (`undated: True`) — not evidence for the accusation and not evidence against it.
- **fleet_mask.py** — Encodes one machine's ledger progress as a bitmask over a shared roster, so two machines can subtract ledgers and name what the other has — without item names crossing the API boundary.
      ↪ encode · decode · compare · load_roster_for · ledger_spec
      ⚠ An ABSENT mask is not an EMPTY one — decode() returns None for absent and a list for present, and a caller that renders them the same will tell him his cousin is missing all 135 pieces the first time the box opens. Every mask carries a 12-char roster sourceHash fingerprint; a decode against a different fingerprint returns UNKNOWN, never a guess. ledger_spec() deep-copies `stores` because dict() is shallow (v2760: a returned spec mutated the module constant process-wide), and an unknown ledger is REFUSED, never defaulted to sets.
- **ledger3.py** — #58 Ledger 3.0 first slice: assembles the per-session EXTRACTION RECORD - what one reel yielded (names, who read each one, on which frames, where it was seen, how it was routed), what the chronicle book banked from it, what the vault ledger witnessed in that visit with the item's tier today, the film or tombstone, the river station and the survey - read-only from the existing stores, for GET /api/ledger3/session and /api/ledger3/sessions and the doctor's lost-trail row.
      ↪ session_record ← GET /api/ledger3/session?id= · sessions ← GET /api/ledger3/sessions · session_summary · session_rows · census ← console_doctor 'ledger3 sessions' · reel_key · name_key
      ⚠ It opens NO file and names NO store: control_app._ledger3_stores hands it the loaded objects (journal ring, chronicle book, vault ledger, shelf path, river stamps, survey, tombstones), each resolved through that module's own path authority, so a fixture never reaches his tree and a law drives the whole record over temp stores. Every key is borrowed, never copied: chronicle_retro._reel_key (one reel, two spellings), item_identity.vault_key + trace_spine.name_key (one name), vault_retro.look_id folded by _fold_bare_sessions (one VISIT per trial, never a frame - his §34.2 ruling), vault_evidence.tier (the one table), chronicle_retro.witnesses (the one independence verdict). A provisional OCR-only name is listed apart and never counted as named (measured: 'Your Gamlng Rlg Is Readyl' x4 rides his journal as names). An unreadable side is None with a sentence in `unknown`, never 0; a session no store knows is ok:False. The reader-vs-vault agreement is two engines shown side by side and never averaged. A tombstone is a release EVENT, not a grave (REG-1557, measured: 3 of his 29 shelf reels carry them, 14 rows between them, every row with a `kept` list, one reel alone four rows): every pass is kept, `tombstone` is the LATEST act (reel_custody's rule for the same store), `releases {n, lastTs, released, kept}` rides beside it and the film says what the passes took; a pass nobody counted leaves `released` UNKNOWN. The two doors answer UNKNOWN for a query they could not read (REG-1556), never '' or a default. It writes nothing; the 📒 room, per-item proof cards, the scenario view and the surgical fixes are later slices (LEDGER3_DESIGN.md).
- **ledger_authority.py** — One manager over sets/uniques/runewords: per-ledger seed vs his own finds, staleness, manual accept/revoke, and the exit path off a seed.
      ↪ authority · classify_row · ledger_figure · exit_path · surface_pairs
      ⚠ `onOwnerSeed` is ONE boolean answering a THREE-ledger question — it is UNSPECIFIC, not over-broad, and cannot say which of sets/uniques/runewords is actually inherited. The two scopes are never mixed: the local board is MEASURED name by name, fleet rows are DERIVED from counts and marked `derived: True`. And the seed totals are read from a live table, not a constant — a hardcoded _GRAIL_SEED goes stale the moment he plays.
- **ledger_highwater.py** — Ratchets a STORED PEAK per ledger key (foundLog, owned, setPieces, rwMade) so the chronicle counter can only go up, and names anything now below its high-water mark.
      ↪ report · latest · historic_peaks · seed · accept
      ⚠ It exists because the doctor compares only the TWO NEWEST snapshots — take three snapshots after a loss and the pairwise diff goes clean while the items are still gone. A peak is not a diff. ⚠ The window BEGINS AFTER the incident that inspired it: snapshots start at foundLog 412, so the 391→383 loss is outside them — a clean window is not a clean history, and it prints that limit rather than filing it away. It shipped GREEN (0 consecutive drops), so it is insurance, not a live bug. `--seed` records today as the peak; `--accept KEY "why"` is the only way to lower one, and requires a reason.
- **ledger_restore.py** — Joins the automatic ledger backup to the board's own apply door: matches a backup by its STAMPED ROUTE, diffs it against the live board, and shapes the gap into `{wouldAdd:{uniques,sets}}`.
      ↪ backups_for · plan · proposal_from
      ⚠ Only foundLog and setPieces can travel back. `owned`, `rwMade` and `gameFound` are BACKED UP but NOT restorable here, and `/api/vault_apply` looks like the door for `owned` and is not — it re-gates any caller-supplied proposal through `_vault_retro()` (3 witnesses, 0.55 confidence floor), a gate added in v1595 that must not be widened for this. Backups written before v2731 carry no route and are SKIPPED with a reason rather than guessed at. Writes nothing; the restore is manual on purpose.
- **main_character.py** — What he WEARS, learned from repeated sightings: names_loc 'equipped' is the only safe equipment signal, and an item locks when wilson_lower(k,n) >= 0.43 over >= 3 sightings.
      ↪ lane_from_sighting(activity,names_loc) · saw() · is_locked() · equipped() · confidence()
      ⚠ EQUIPMENT_ALIASES accepts BOTH 'equipment' and 'equipped' because the scene classifier emits neither — accepting one spelling is how a fed learner still pins equip at 0; inventory_law wins outright, so the Horadric Cube is locked at zero sightings.
- **provenance.py** — The one writer-side call that stamps a store with WHO produced it and at what version (`_prov`), atomically; load_json reads that back.
      ↪ save_json · load_json · stamp · read · classify
      ⚠ `by` is a required argument with no default, no stack walk and no __name__ sniffing, and by='unknown' is refused BY NAME. An unavailable version writes `verUnknown: <why>` and NO `ver` key — never null, never the string 'unknown' — so classify() grades it PARTIAL. Reading a store that predates the stamp does not raise: it returns known=False with the payload intact. This is the MEDICINE; verdict_provenance.py is the census that only reports.
- **read_identity.py** — One extractor of a read's identity signals (game name and farming series, the login character, the character panel, the avatar class) that sessions, the River, the Vault evidence and the Fleet consume.
      ↪ identity_ledger · who_at · game_at · attribute_finds · finds_index · character_signals · game_runs · say_games
      ⚠ A conflict between signals is kept side by side and the attribution is UNKNOWN, never a guess; the game password is cut before the password word and never leaves this module.
- **roster_routes.py** — A21c — discovers every *_roster.json catalog on disk and traces its chain (declared in bible.html → getter on window → probe asks it → total on the wire → a surface states its unit), flagging any break.
      ↪ routes
      ⚠ The docstring's "⚠ JOIN NOT LANDED IN THIS FILE" is STALE — the join HAS landed: control_app.roster_route_state() feeds heart_state()["rosters"] and run_gates.py registers the test_roster_routes Gate. route_totals is imported DEFENSIVELY behind a _NoProducer stand-in, so a tree where the producer will not import yields UNKNOWN totals rather than a lane inventing one. Corroboration reuses chronicle_routes.corroborate — the same function, not a copy. Routes are discovered from disk artifacts because a hand-kept getter table is how _gSetRoster sat named with no definition for months.
- **roster_sync.py** — Regenerates unique_roster.json / set_roster.json from the live bible.html over CDP, and stamps a sourceHash that a test verifies without launching a browser.
      ↪ is_stale · source_hash · load · fetch_via_cdp · fetch_sets_via_cdp
      ⚠ The roster rule is deliberately NOT re-derived in Python — it loads the real page and asks `window._gUniqueRoster()`, so there is only one authority. Only `is_stale()`/`source_hash()` run browserless; `--write` needs Chrome on :9224+ (never :9222 his, never :9223 TradingView).
- **route_totals.py** — The one reader that says what a chronicle tab COUNTS — runeword 99, set 135, unique 403 — read out of bible.html, and None when unreadable.
      ↪ total · noun · why · canonical · disagreements
      ⚠ 105 and 398 are ARTIFACT LENGTHS (roster row counts, incl. 4 bare runeword spellings), never totals — demoted but deliberately kept, so the artifacts become checked copies rather than denominators. Every failure returns None, never a fallback constant. Not the same file as tv/chronicle_total.py, which BANKS the dated CASC measurement this one merely quotes.
- **sets_base_index.py** — Maps a BASE item name back to the SET PIECES still to find, the sets-side counterpart of bible.html's _chUniquesOnBase, so a reader can keyword-search for what is left.
      ↪ build · coverage · main
      ⚠ The uniques mechanism could NOT be copied: ITEM_CODEX carries a `base` for only 14 of the 135 set pieces. And the slot suffix is NOT the base — sometimes it is (Taebaek's Glory (ward) → Ward), sometimes it is a CATEGORY (Natalya's Mark (claws) → Scissors Suwayyah, IK Soul Cage (armor) → Sacred Armor), so a suffix rule would silently mis-resolve. It only covers pieces on a recorded Remaining page — 19 of 135 today — and coverage() states that fraction rather than letting a caller assume completeness (a base on the Remaining page is by definition a piece he does not have, so that is the set worth expanding).

### machine-tree (1)

- **machine_tree.py** — Answers where THIS machine's roots are and whether a write actually lands in them, so a console that has never recorded can be told apart from one that is broken. It reports; it does not provision by being looked at.
      ↪ resolve() — this machine's roots, each carrying the anchor that produced it · ensure(create=False) — establish the tree and say what it did · anchors() — env / repo / home, each with WHY it resolved that way · _prove_write() — a directory that EXISTS is not a directory that WORKS · ROOTS — 5 roots, each declaring its OWN anchor kind
      ⚠ It is NOT a fix for a provisioning defect - there is none. tv_diablo makes the footage tree inside _film_loop, so a console that has never filmed has no frames/hist BY DESIGN, and frame_authority/reel_retention are READERS whose 0 makedirs is correct. Creating a tree in the WRONG place is worse than creating none: it turns the heart green and makes the fault invisible, so a refused anchor yields NO path rather than a guess. found requires a PROVEN WRITE, a frozen build refuses the repo anchor, and every path is _ascii()-safe because a non-ASCII profile path crashes a cp1255 console WHILE it reports.

### push (2)

- **console_settle.py** — Let his console finish moving onto the pushed code before the pre-push demos drive it (v3526 push #1 was refused by a demo 7 s after the re-exec).
      ↪ wait_until_settled · verdict · read · main
      ⚠ Called by hooks/pre-push right before the console demos (#42c): it waits, bounded at 360 s, while his console runs older code than the file on disk and its relaunch is not held, relaunched under 30 s ago, or reports its engine not ready. A held relaunch, no answer or the bound all go on - it never refuses a push; the demos decide.
- **push_lane.py** — Decides which lane a push takes for hooks/pre-push: ci-only (every ref is refs/heads/ci/*, graded on GitHub, nothing deploys) or full (the whole Mac gate).
      ↪ lane · main
      ⚠ It fails toward the FULL gate: one ref that is not refs/heads/ci/<name>, any ref naming refs/heads/main (even a deletion), no ref that ships, a malformed line, or the script failing all mean full. It reads the refs git hands the hook, never the branch the shell stands on.

### render-pixels (13)

- **a11y_check.py** — CDP-probes the live bible.html DOM tab by tab and names every control that has no accessible name; exits 2 UNKNOWN when it cannot look.
      ↪ main
      ⚠ A `placeholder` is deliberately NOT counted as a name (it vanishes the moment you type) — that is exactly the weaker question the static guard in test_control.py was asking while staying green over 21 fields. No Chrome / wrong tab / no controls returns exit 2 UNKNOWN, never a green line. Nothing imports or runs it; test_control.py only lists the filename in a duplicate-top-level-def sweep.
- **crest_loudness.py** — Measures, by SATURATED-PIXEL SHARE, whether the install crest out-shouts everything else above the fold on bible.html at 1440x1000 — a visual-priority gate.
      ↪ main
      ⚠ Its own docstring is STALE about the refusal: it says 'NO CHROME IS UNKNOWN… Exit 2 says nobody looked', but since v2430 no-Chrome returns 77 (SKIP, which run_gates counts apart from PASS and FAIL and requires declared in skip_ok=); exit 2 now only means the harness/PIL would not load. Ranking is by saturated-pixel DENSITY, not count or chroma×area — both of those simply elected the page's largest containers. It claims board ownership (`d2r_ownerClaim`) before measuring because a CDP probe boots as a GUEST with seeds suppressed.
- **dom_probe.py** — JS probe source strings (__leafText/__clipped/__covers/__painted/__quoted) that stop a DOM measurement answering an adjacent question.
      ↪ prelude
      ⚠ It exports JS SOURCE, not Python behaviour — prelude() returns a string you prepend to a page evaluation; the only Python def in the file is prelude(). The five scars it encodes: `body *` includes <script>, a clip test on an INLINE element is meaningless (clientWidth 0), occlusion has a direction, selecting by text finds the wrong box, and never infer colour from a class name.
- **frozen_frame_watch.py** — Detects a dead-but-200 console from the PIXELS — two consecutive byte-identical captures of the same window geometry means the screen stopped painting.
      ↪ report · frames · png_geometry · shelf_dir
      ⚠ GROUP BY GEOMETRY or the verdict is meaningless — the shelf holds 383 PNGs across eight geometries (full windows AND header/footer/rail crops), so 'hash the newest N files' compares a header crop against a footer crop and reports NOT FROZEN from a comparison that could never go red. It also does NOT take rows[1]: it takes the newest frame MIN_GAP_S or more older, because a copied file is identical by definition. It says FROZEN, never 'blank' — blank is a content claim only an eye can make. No frames = UNKNOWN, never healthy.
- **frozen_frames.py** — Reads capture files for two separate states: FROZEN (repeated sha256 of bytes) and BLANK (decoded pixels judged by paint_witness).
      ↪ scan · look · shot · frozen_runs · captures
      ⚠ FROZEN and BLANK are never merged, and collapsing them is the defect. A repeated hash is a POINTER, not a verdict — it says the feed stopped moving and says nothing about what is on screen; a window can be frozen on a perfectly painted frame, and blank while its titlebar clock still ticks so every frame differs. The blankness bar is quoted from `paint_witness.verdict()`, never re-typed here.
- **overlap_ratchet.py** — Counts hit-tested text-on-text overlaps per page per viewport width and grades them as a ratchet against a recorded baseline — the class render_check cannot see.
      ↪ measure · check · write_baseline · main
      ⚠ Overlaps are hit-tested with elementFromPoint, NOT bounding rects — rects reported 21 of 24 phantom collisions inside a clipped `#home-dash`, and a desktop 'finding' written up from them did not exist. It is a ratchet, so a FALL fails too until blessed; the 2 overlaps at 1120 in the baseline are recorded DEBT, not judged harmless.
- **paint_witness.py** — Reads the compositor's bitmap of his console window and answers BLANK / PAINTED / UNKNOWN from modal-colour share — it never asks the page.
      ↪ look · verdict · blank_strikes · rescue_worked · console_pid
      ⚠ Darkness is NOT the test — 72.7% of his healthy dark-theme pixels are below luminance 24, so a darkness check would call a working console blank every run; UNIFORMITY (modal share) is the verdict. The second bar `distinct<=4` was deliberately REMOVED after it passed a blank-white window, because chrome alone contributes 8-9 luminances. It is a witness, not a trigger: nothing here reloads, restarts or writes, and its most valuable answer is the refusal that forbids a rescue.
- **pixel_witness_live.py** — Self-arming seat console.pixel_rescue — looks at HIS on-screen console 8x; each PAINTED verdict banks a refused false-blank attack.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ run · main
      ⚠ Nothing imports it — it is wired by NAME in self_arming.py:484 ('pixel_witness_live': ('console.pixel_rescue',)) as a PROVES allow-list entry. UNKNOWN is NOT a refusal of the false-blank attack: it means nobody looked. Only PAINTED counts, so the seat is only meaningful with his console actually on screen; Grok Bot fills the seat by looking, Claude only owns the file.
- **region_witness.py** — Grid paint witness: measures an N×M grid of the console window and reports which CELLS are drawn vs blank, so 'this half is painted and that half is not' is expressible.
      ↪ cells · regions · half_blank · half_blank_strikes
      ⚠ `half_blank` returns half=False in BOTH the fully-painted and the fully-BLANK case — a whole-window blank is paint_witness's question, not this one — so a caller reading `half` as 'is the window ok' gets it backwards on a dead window. It is a READING, not a fault: a legitimately empty panel is blank and fine, and the doctor row decides. Named regions are DERIVED from grid columns, never measured separately, so the two can never disagree; a grid was chosen over two named panels precisely so today's layout is not hardcoded into a health check. READ-ONLY on his surface — never focuses, raises, resizes, clicks or reloads. HALF_STRIKES=3, because one frame is a sample.
- **render_bound.py** — REG-1993 - the factor the render gate's three coupled numbers grow by under load: the hook's kill, render_check._CLEAN_RUN_COST and _RUN_REPORT_BY. load / 5 (the daytime load they were measured at), never below 1, capped at 2.5; the hook measures it when the render starts and hands it down as GATE_SCALE / TV_RENDER_SCALE.
      ↪ scale · from_env · main
      ⚠ Not a higher fixed ceiling - that is an absent hang detector on every quiet push. A hand run of render_check reads no factor and keeps 317 / 333; the hook's literal 353 stays the measured base so the laws that read it are unchanged.
- **render_check.py** — The render loop: boots a private headless Chrome on :9224, seeds and activates each registered TARGET, measures clipped / zero-size / overlap / painted at several widths, and refuses rather than passing.
      ↪ check · verdict · prove · main
      ⚠ A SKIP IS NOT A PASS — no Chrome, no websocket client, or no target found returns UNKNOWN and exits 2 on purpose. A zero-size element and a black capture are REFUSALS, not readings, because '0 clipped' on an inactive tab is a false green. `verdict()` is split out pure so the suite can prove the refusals BEHAVIOURALLY instead of grepping this file for its own sentences, and refusals-vs-notes are two separate lists (a `ⓘ` sniff used to decide blocking). Port is 9224 — never his 9222 or TV's 9223. A target may name its own page and may ask to be `serve:`d.
- **stage_witness.py** — Holds the DOM's claim and the actual PIXELS side by side for the console room — dom_claim() reads /api/status, pixel_claim() grids the live window — and publishes the DISAGREEMENT.
      ↪ dom_claim · pixel_claim · verdict · report
      ⚠ `painted` and `ink` in the beat are DOM measurements wearing pixel names — 3,090 shelf cards built, painted:true, ink:true, while he photographed a black room. The grid is 8x5 because at the previously shipped 3x2 every cell catches a lit edge (header/footer/rail) and all six read PAINTED over a dead room; cells at or right of RAIL_FROM_COL=5 are excluded because the rail stays lit; MIN_BLANK_CELLS is 2 (his own dead room produced exactly 2, so it is not a number chosen to pass). Neither side alone is evidence — the finding is the contradiction. It READS AND NEVER ACTS: no relaunch, no repair, no click.
- **window_visibility.py** — Asks the OS (CGWindowList) whether his console window is really on screen and whether another app is covering ≥95% of it — an independent witness the page's own heartbeat cannot supply.
      ↪ on_screen · covered_by · contradicts_a_hidden_beat
      ⚠ THREE ANSWERS, NEVER TWO: None = could not establish (no Quartz, an exception, no window of ours), and None must never read as False — 'nobody looked' and 'it is not there' are opposite facts. It exists because document.visibilityState is CIRCULAR: WebKit throttles a hidden window's timers and the heartbeat IS a timer, so from the first hidden beat the rescue is disarmed forever. Window LISTING needs no Screen Recording grant — only capturing pixels does — which is why it is not screenshot-based on this Mac. REG-594: a window listed on screen and covered 100% by Citrix Viewer used to return True and reloaded a healthy console seven times in one day; a covered window CONFIRMS a hidden beat. And UNKNOWN occlusion is not permission — it returns False.

### resume-state (1)

- **resume_state.py** — Derives the live half of RESUME_HERE.md so a resume cannot go stale, the way BLUEPRINT.md and HEART.md cannot. CLAUDE.md points every new session at that file FIRST, and on 2026-09-20 it was FOUR DAYS OLD, opening with 6 commits are built and UNPUSHED while naming five that had shipped long before. Nothing noticed because nothing could: it was prose.
      ↪ derived() — the state block: HEAD, origin, unpushed, tree, version, gates · write(path) — replace the block between the markers, in place · --check — exit non-zero when the fingerprint no longer matches the repo · BEGIN / END — the markers; everything outside them is hand-written narrative
      ⚠ It NEVER fetches. origin/main is whatever the last fetch left in the ref, so the block SAYS how old that knowledge is rather than implying it is current - a generator that reached the network would make every bump wait on it and could hang a ship. Staleness is judged on a FINGERPRINT OF THE FACTS (head, origin, version), never the whole block, because the block also prints a derive timestamp and a fetch age that move every minute and a gate that is always red is switched off as fast as one that is always green. dirty is excluded on purpose: uncommitted files are ordinary mid-work. The narrative OUTSIDE the markers is never touched - a resume that is only derived state cannot say WHY anything is blocked. And it prints non-ASCII, so it enables console_safe or it crashes a cp1255 console WHILE reporting.

### retention (1)

- **journal_drain.py** — The WRITER half of the journal drain: it backs up every generation of the session journal, removes the rows a plan released, and carries forward anything the recorder appended while it was writing. It exists as its own module because test_a_journal_row_leaves_only_on_proof forbids the PLANNER from holding any write-capable call, so the planner decides and this acts.
      ↪ apply_plan · backup
      ⚠ It refuses far more often than it writes, and every refusal is deliberate. It will not run without an explicit yes, will not run if a backup did not verify row-for-row, will not run if the corpus moved since the plan judged it, and will not rename its rewrite over a file whose INODE changed — tv_diablo rotates the ring with os.replace, which makes sessions.1.jsonl GROW, so a line count cannot see it. Identity comes from os.fstat of the handle it read, never from the path.

### river-reels (35)

- **chronicle_retro.py** — Retro Chronicle sweep engine — groups sealed-reel frames into still runs, reads only the Chronicle pages, returns a merge-max grail proposal.
      ↪ sweep_hist · read_reel · proposal_from_pages · merge_proposals · apply_proposal
      ⚠ LAW 1: this module cannot write AT ALL, and test_chronicle_retro proves it from the source text — which is why index recovery lives in reel_index.py and persistence in shadow_ledger.py. Also: STILL_MAX_DIFF=0.22 does NOT keep a scrolling read in one run (it shatters it); `known_chronicle` is the door that rescues those frames.
- **chronicle_sweep_now.py** — CLI that runs control_app's own chronicle sweep over unread reels without opening the console; capped at --max reels, no re-arming loop.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ main
      ⚠ No module imports it — it is the command-line twin of the in-console sweep watchdog, and test_control gates its SOURCE TEXT for copy-drift against control_app's rule. Without --yes it only PRICES the work and exits; it proposes names and never applies them; and since v1832 a LIVE capture session is no longer a refusal (it skips only the one reel still growing).
- **declared_vs_content.py** — A15 — checks that a reel's route is derived from its CONTENT, never from the declared `focus` stamp in index.json.
      ↪ report · main
      ⚠ Its verdict today is UNTESTABLE, not CLEAN — measured on his tree: 40 reels, 1 declaring a chronicle focus, that one carrying 0 surveyed panels, 0 disagreements. Zero disagreements over an unexercised sample measures the SAMPLE, not the routing law. Two sites DO route on a declared stamp and are NOT defects: chronicle_retro._declared_kind (the declaration picks the reader, the sweep then judges content) and reel_retention._vault_lane_owes (an ABSENT stamp HOLDS the reel — errs toward keeping).
- **end_routes.py** — The end-route predicate reverse-engineered from the 410 tombstoned reels: three independent doors (structural, semantic, unextracted), read-only.
      ↪ verdict · report · derived_from · reprocessing_list · sources
      ⚠ Every one of the 410 tombstones records why: 'read (N pages) and sealed by BOTH lanes' — and for 395 of them BOTH clauses are FALSE. 96.6% of all end-route traffic went through the STRUCTURAL door, which means 'there is nothing here to read' (394 had pages:0, 392 had no vault seal), so the permanent record of an irreversible delete says the opposite of what happened. The doors are a DISJUNCTION and must stay one: conjoining them would have refused 96% of every journey ever taken. The header names only two doors; `unextracted_door` is a third, added 2026-09-13. It decides nothing and deletes nothing — a second prune authority is precisely what it refuses to be. HELD never hides an unasked door: `unasked` is reported with each gap measured None.
- **engine_joins.py** — Census of which per-reel engines are actually consulted by printer.py and which answer nobody — joinedness DERIVED by walking printer.py's own AST imports, never a hand-kept flag.
      ↪ census · main
      ⚠ It proves a FIELD ARRIVED, not that this engine put it there — with no per-field provenance a JOINED means 'the answer is on the row'. And 'unjoined' is two different problems: NO_DATA names engines whose fix is CAPTURE not wiring (slot_identity: 0 of 1,065 deep rows carry a slot; main_character: he does not film the character panel), while WHY_NOT records engines deliberately outside the printer (reel_segments and frame_authority are joined INDIRECTLY and a second import would be drift). HUB_FANOUT=15 excludes control_app's 65 imports from making everything look joined.
- **extract_gap.py** — Per reel, why extraction is blocked and how much gap is RECOVERABLE — seals lacking names the journal already read. It writes nothing.
      ↪ gap · holding_possible
      ⚠ It never writes, and especially never a seal — back-filling one would forge the certification frame_authority exists to protect. And 'the names exist' is NOT 'a holding exists': `holding_possible()` is the law that decides which read names the extraction contract can actually take, and on his store it removes the loudest row on the report (45 Chronicle checklist entries). Any RECOVERABLE figure read without it is inflated.
- **frame_authority.py** — The ONE deletion authority over his footage - the FRAME deleter asks IS THIS FRAME EVIDENCE, and an unreadable store, unparsable name or missing index all resolve to KEEP.
      ↪ frame_verdict() · plan_frames() · seal_verdict() · seal_releases_frames() · witness_index()
      ⚠ It REPORTS rather than acts (:450 'this replaces a deleter that ran automatically'); sig_diff was replaced, not retuned — three frames naming three different grand charms are pairwise 0.00000 apart, so a hover tooltip moves it by literally zero.
- **frame_ref.py** — The one resolver for the three shapes a frame is addressed by: journal frameId 21_1787522052389, top-level f_<ms>.jpg, and reel-relative <reel>/f_<ms>.jpg. A failed match is None, never 'deleted'.
      ↪ timestamp_of() · reel_of() · stem_of() · cited_frames() · prunable()
      ⚠ None from timestamp_of means NOT ESTABLISHED, not 1970: a reel's 5-digit random suffix and a 1-2 digit index are rejected by the 12-14 digit epoch window, and a `15_1787496136628#v` suffix is still read by taking the leading digit run.
- **one_funnel.py** — The 6-rung stage ladder and whether reels actually flowed down it — occupancy plus the dated waypoints, which cover only 2 of the 6 rungs.
      ↪ funnel · waypoints
      ⚠ `stage` is a BLOCKER, not a trajectory — `reel_story._stage_of` maps a reel's current HOLD TAG to the rung it is stuck BEFORE. So 'no reel sits at banked' and 'no reel ever passed banked' are opposite facts and the field cannot tell them apart; reading occupancy as a route is the measured-true-read-wrong defect. Only `retro_triage` (40/40) and `vault_swept` (15/40) leave a dated waypoint, so THE PASSAGE is partial by construction.
- **one_start_point.py** — Asks the SHELF — the reels themselves, not a source grep — which front door each reel entered by: the recorder's core record, the repair door, or the fixture door.
      ↪ start_points · main
      ⚠ Three writers exist and only one is a front door — `reel_index.ensure_reel_index` (repair) and `vault_fixture_reels` (fixture) mint no reel from footage, so reporting them as violations would be crying wolf. A repaired index is genuinely THINNER (no per-frame `blank` flags, since (f, ts) is derived from filenames) but the shelf refutes the harm: only 3 of 40 reels carry a blank flag at all. Every return, including UNKNOWN, carries the same keys (REG-546).
- **orphan_fold.py** — Clusters loose f_<ms>.jpg frames left by a session that DIED BEFORE IT SEALED and folds them into a proper reel_s_<t0>_<n> directory, so footage no lane can see stops being invisible.
      ↪ plan · apply_plan · main
      ⚠ A cluster whose time window overlaps ANY existing reel is REPORTED AND NEVER MOVED — folding leftovers of an already-sealed session into a new directory would mint a NEW sessionId (reel_index derives sessionId from the directory name) for pixels from one recording, forging exactly the independence the keep gate counts. The directory is named reel_s_… not reel_orphan_… because the v2065 recording parser is ^s_(\d{10,16}), and an orphan-named reel reads as UNKNOWN time. It moves and never deletes, and refuses to act without --yes.
- **per_reel_routes.py** — Attributes each reel's route to what DECIDED it — policy (age or test-fixture), a global fact about the tree, or the reel's own content.
      ↪ routes · main
      ⚠ The finding is not a defect and must not be reported as one. Measured on his 40 reels the two columns are the SAME 28 and the SAME 12: every reel that reached the far end got there BY POLICY, and every content-routed reel is parked at one rung under one tag (`zero-pages`). So the content-earned divergence A15 asks for exists in the code and NOTHING ON HIS SHELF EXERCISES IT — UNEXERCISED, which is a third fact distinct from both working and broken. Also note printer.py's scar: story() was being run twice, once via reel_river.river() and once via routes(), each doing its own reel_retention.plan().
- **printer.py** — THE 3D/4D PRINTER — follows every reel through seven stations (in · funnel · template · route · extract · out · tombstone), each answer QUOTED from the module that owns that question.
      ↪ stream · counts_for · main
      ⚠ It PRINTS NOTHING AND DELETES NOTHING — it is a report and no station may refuse or remove. The `out` station is deliberately UNDECIDED: the REEL door and the FRAME contract disagree (12 of 40 finished vs 0 of 15 asked) and conjoining them is exactly what v2312 attempted and v2314 withdrew. Count subsets with counts_for() rather than re-tallying — the console's spine once read 24 stations over a 16-reel shelf because it re-derived the aggregate.
- **printer_reach.py** — Asks how much of the sealed corpus the printer pipeline can act on under EXTRACTION_CONTRACT: CLEAN, CONTRADICTION, UNREACHABLE or UNKNOWN.
      ↪ report · main
      ⚠ Its zero contradictions is NOT a healthy pipeline: 0 of 30 seals satisfy the contract, so the A4 contradiction is STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE — a count taken through a filter that rejects every input measures the filter. UNREACHABLE is a distinct verdict from CLEAN on purpose. A retracted line once claimed all 22 failed on `name` alone; that was this module's own `str(cwhy)[:70]` truncation collapsing every distinct refusal into one bucket — re-measured, name, location AND provenance are missing on all 30. And missing `name` on a grid-only reel is DESIGN ('film cannot name grids'), not a defect to fix here.
- **prune_shadow.py** — Scores what Wilson WOULD say about a prune pass (k = candidates with a demonstrably live OCR reader over n considered). Decides nothing.
      ↪ score · tags_for · floors_are_reachable · say · main
      ⚠ It deletes nothing, arms nothing and does NOT bank into self_arming — it only records how far off arming is. It cannot unblock the architectural fault it names: _GATE_SILENT/_GATE_HEARD are PROCESS GLOBALS in control_app, so a concurrent sweep can collapse a tooltip frame into the deletable class; this measures confidence given honest inputs, it does not make dishonest inputs honest. Confluence is taken over the WEAKEST candidate, never the average. No production module imports it — only gates do.
- **reel_custody.py** — #55 first slice — ONE reel's chain of custody: which of five hands (recorder, triage, printer, vault, tombstone) hold a record of it, each quoted from the store that hand wrote, the river's journey beside them, and every reel on which two writers contradict each other. Behind /api/custody and the doctor row 'reel custody'.
      ↪ custody · census · doctor · sources · world
      ⚠ It RE-DERIVES NOTHING: the four dict stores come through end_routes.sources (which asks retro_triage and reel_retention for their paths), every keyed-both-ways lookup is reel_retention.lookup_either_way, a seal's meaning is frame_authority.seal_verdict, the journey is river_stamp.history, the door a tombstoned reel left by is end_routes.verdict. A hand is held True / False / None and the three are three: None (the store exists and will not read) never becomes False, and a contradiction is NEVER raised on a None side. The census lists no reel's frames (a listing per reel on every doctor tick is the a_watcher_over_a_growing_folder scar); only the per-reel record does. `reads` and `plan` are handed IN by the caller (the console's journal and reel_retention.plan) — None means not asked, never 'no reads'. MEASURED 2026-09-29 before it existed: 460 tombstones all say 'sealed by BOTH lanes', 44 have a vault seal, 56 have neither, 0 river stamps say TOMBSTONE.
- **reel_demo.py** — Walks his REAL reels through every printer station and checks downstream against route_totals' REGISTERED numbers. Reads, never writes.
      ↪ demo · main
      ⚠ Nothing imports it — run_gates.py:3410 registers it as Gate('reel_demo'). It is a DEMONSTRATION, not a unit test: it asserts against route_totals' registered runewords/sets/uniques rather than re-deriving them from the code under test. With no shelf it exits 77 (declared SKIP, venue 'no-shelf'); a shelf that EXISTS but walks nothing still fails, because an earlier version exited 0 having walked nothing.
- **reel_door.py** — Which DOOR opened each reel - onair, shadow or mini - read once from the play journal so the river and the shelf quote the same answer instead of each rolling its own.
      ↪ door_map · door_of · split · say
      ⚠ ⚠ A DOORLESS REEL IS UNKNOWN, NEVER THE COMMON DOOR. 16 of his reels predate the v2687 stamp and carry no door row; door_of() returns None for them and split() keeps `unknown` as its own bucket, because a manufactured provenance cannot be told from a real one afterwards. ⚠ THE FIELD IS `door`, NOT `origin` - tv_diablo already uses `origin` for the dispatch context (settle/heartbeat/text-eye/farewell), a different question. ⚠ An UNREADABLE journal returns ({}, why) and the why is what separates 'nobody recorded a door' from 'nobody could look'. ⚠ Pure stdlib and no console import, so the laws can drive it with a throwaway journal.
- **reel_index.py** — THE ONLY WRITER of a reel's index.json — self-heals a reel whose index is missing or corrupt by rebuilding it from the f_<epoch-ms>.jpg frame names, atomically.
      ↪ ensure_reel_index
      ⚠ It is a separate file purely so chronicle_retro keeps its structural read-only proof (test_chronicle_retro asserts write-freedom from SOURCE) — do not move this function next door. It returns a GOOD index UNCHANGED (never rewritten, reordered or enriched), preserves a corrupt one as index.json.corrupt before replacing it, and refuses any directory not named reel_* — otherwise cache160/cache1280 thumbnail dirs, which are also full of f_<ms>.jpg, get 'healed' into fake reels that theatre then lists as footage. A failed write is LOUD and returns None; its print prefix still says "chronicle_retro:".
- **reel_repair.py** — Surveys tv/frames/hist for sealed reels whose index.json is missing or unparseable and rebuilds it from the frame filenames — dry-run by default, --apply writes.
      ↪ survey · inspect · repair · read_index · main
      ⚠ Blank flags are NOT reconstructed, on purpose: a guessed `blank:true` makes a sweep skip a frame and hides real footage forever, and the per-frame JPEG decode (0.076 s/frame) is the exact expense that lost the index in the first place. The rebuild itself is delegated to reel_index.ensure_reel_index() — never re-implemented — which is why a second --apply is idempotent. A CORRUPT index is copied to `.bad-<epoch>` before replacement, a reel with a FRESH `index.json.tmp` (younger than STALE_TMP_S) is SKIPPED as a seal in flight, and an older one is an ABANDONED seal that repair finishes - its temp installed when it lists at least what index.json lists, otherwise kept aside (REG-1679).
- **reel_retention.py** — The REEL deleter: which whole reels have given up their information and may go, behind five bars (evidence banked, both lanes sealed, newest 8 kept, oldest first, stop at the floor).
      ↪ plan() · apply_plan() (--apply --yes) · proof_reels() · _tombstone() · KEEP_RECENT = 8 (his 2026-09-10 ruling) · blocked_upstream() - the river's last stamps vs the pass's plan: what waits UPSTREAM of the mouth (REG-1517) · drain_state() - the drain in the shared vocabulary; BLOCKED when reels older than the newest window wait upstream
      ⚠ A 0-page seal means THIS READER FOUND NOTHING, not done — 'delete what has been swept' would have taken 1166 MB that was never read; the single removing line is one shutil.rmtree at :1101 and it needs --apply AND --yes. REG-1517: drain_owed() counts the plan's CANDIDATES - reels held at EMPTY or PRINTER are not candidates, so on the ALT (EMPTY 91 / PRINTER 33) the drain said CLEAR 'nothing is owed'; drain_state now takes the river's own reading (blocked_upstream) and a CLEAR needs a readable, complete river - unread is UNKNOWN, never CLEAR.
- **reel_river.py** — A10 — walks one reel through every stage (filmed/triaged/swept/banked/vault-done/releasable) and names the DECIDER and the QUESTION each stage answered.
      ↪ river · main
      ⚠ "12 reels RELEASABLE" vs "0 seals satisfy frame_authority's EXTRACTION_CONTRACT" is NOT a contradiction and the module exists because it read as one — frame_authority answers may this FRAME go (protecting witness frames behind vault rows), reel_retention answers may this REEL go. Two authorities at two granularities is correct; the v2312 attempt to collapse them was WITHDRAWN. A disagreement is only called a GAP when two deciders answer the SAME question differently.
- **reel_route_lane.py** — The river's outlet — stamps EMPTY reels ROUTED without deleting bytes, and publishes its refusal of the CAPTURE reels that owe a capture first.
      ↪ plan · apply · assert_matches_owes · main
      ⚠ ROUTED (a statement about the DATA) and TOMBSTONE (a statement about the BYTES) are different facts — this writes the first and never the second, so a reel can finish the river with its footage untouched behind the still-locked deleter. `apply()` is the ONLY state change here and the only `may("reel.route")` call site; `plan()` writes nothing. `declined` is not an error list.
- **reel_router.py** — Assigns each reel on the shelf exactly ONE station from its own evidence (surveyed / names read / sealed), never from the retention tag.
      ↪ route · roster · owed · labels · assert_independent_of_retention
      ⚠ The separation from retention IS the module: reel_story._stage_of(tag) let 'do we keep these bytes' decide 'where is this reel in the river', which starved 29 of 40 reels because the vault-owes tag is last in a first-match-wins list and fired 0 times. assert_independent_of_retention() is the executable guard, and route() ASSERTS sum(counts)==shelf so a silently dropped reel cannot hide; an UNKNOWN walk still publishes `closed`, because a missing key reads as nothing.
- **reel_segments.py** — A reel is a timeline: deep `scene` reads grouped into activity segments (gameplay/stash/chronicle/town) so a judged name can be asked what was on screen at that moment.
      ↪ segments(rows,gap_ms) · activity_at() · lane_at_graded() · corroborates() · corroborates_chrome()
      ⚠ No padding by default — +/-30s lifts coverage from 26 to 56 of 84 but 20 of the extra land in 'gameplay', which is an absence of a claim rather than a rebuttal; only DEEP reads carry a scene, so the OCR lane cannot define a segment.
- **reel_story.py** — Where each reel stands on the six-stage ladder (filmed → triaged → swept → banked → vault-done → releasable) plus how much of its footage was worth keeping; it reads the stage from reel_retention.plan()'s own tag.
      ↪ story · main
      ⚠ IT DECIDES NOTHING — a second opinion here would be a second prune authority over footage with no un-delete. A reel that has not REACHED a stage is 'not yet', never 'failed'; and yield None ('nobody surveyed it') must not collapse into yield 0.0 ('surveyed, carried nothing'). TAG_STAGE is TAUGHT, not guessed — an unknown tag makes _stage_of REFUSE rather than guess — and two entries are counter-intuitive: `panels-never-banked` is stuck before BANKED (not swept: the survey DID find the panels), and `holds-proof` maps to RELEASABLE because it fires last in plan()'s chain. POLICY_HOLDS vs GLOBAL_HOLDS must not be painted the same colour.
- **reel_templates.py** — What a reel IS (reel_segments activity) and therefore its route/zone — the printer's template layer, plus prune CANDIDATES on paper only.
      ↪ templates · routes_of · route_census
      ⚠ UNKNOWN is NEVER a prune candidate — a reel the segmenter cannot classify has not been shown irrelevant, it has not been read, and collapsing those routes unexamined footage to the deleter. It deletes nothing: a prune candidate is a row on paper and `prune` stays off by its own flag. It invents no vocabulary — activities are reel_segments', the lane map is `reel_segments._ACTIVITY_LANE`, the ring is `control_app._journal_ring()`; a second copy of any of those is copy-drift.
- **retro_triage.py** — Free, AI-free pixel triage of unread reels — decides which hold an open panel worth paying to read, remembers per-reel hit counts, deletes nothing.
      ↪ survey · remember · worth_reading · owed · unread_reels
      ⚠ Its own cost figure is CONTRADICTED on purpose and both readings are kept: 0.486 s/frame and ~0.019 s/frame. Plan on ~0.02; 0.486 is unexplained, not authoritative. And the panel hit rate is NOT established (samples gave 2% and 20%), so no sample may size the backlog — only a full pass produces a disposal list. It runs throttled (`budget_s`, `nice_delay_s`) so it does not make his game stutter.
- **river.py** — Walks eleven joints capture->survey->read->gate->seal->prune->disk and grades each CARRIES/STARVED/DRY/UNKNOWN, naming the first blockage.
      ↪ trace · summary · main
      ⚠ The API is trace() + summary(rows) — NOT survey()/run(); console_doctor's first cut guessed those two names behind a hasattr and would have returned UNKNOWN forever while looking wired. DRY (nothing crossed WHILE material waited upstream) versus STARVED (nothing upstream) is the entire instrument — collapsing them is how every unjoined end hid. It reports and never applies: the apply stays his.
- **river_epoch.py** — Drives his real reels down the river in cycles until nothing moves, to fire reel_retention's 7 never-fired rules - eligible, holds-proof, vault-owes and friends. Re-admits only; deletes nothing.
      ↪ run() · snapshot() · pass_once() · never_fired() · moved()
      ⚠ `eligible` has NEVER fired on his footage — not once has a reel been ruled safe to release — which is why deletion stays off this lane. Its only action is vault_reentry_sweep (dry by default), and both callees report failure as a payload, not an exception, so an unreadable river once read as a measured zero.
- **river_lanes.py** — Groups reel_router.route()'s nine stations into the four lanes he named — INTAKE, PRINTER, CAPTURE, TOMBSTONE — as a VIEW of the backend, computing no station of its own.
      ↪ lanes · assert_partitions · main
      ⚠ assert_partitions() runs FIRST, before the router is even walked, because the partition is a property of the CODE not the data — it used to run below the router check, so on any machine whose walk came back UNKNOWN the structural check never ran at all. Every station belongs to exactly one lane, held against reel_router.STATIONS rather than a copy, so a tenth station upstream turns this RED instead of vanishing. FIFO is INHERITED from route() and never re-sorted (None capture-time goes LAST, not 1970-first). `hide` is the CALLER's set, applied before grouping so shelf/counts/byStation/reconciles all move together. TOMBSTONE only became reachable at v2764 when ROUTED was unwelded from the deleter. SHOW_CLOSED=24 caps rows DRAWN, never the count.
- **river_stamp.py** — The only store that REMEMBERS a reel's journey: append-only stamps of each station reached, by whom, why. No trim; the ceiling refuses.
      ↪ stamp · run · history · current · census · last_stamps · positions · close_out
      ⚠ Append-only means no second mode: the store opens 'a' and never 'w', there is no age-trim, and at _CEILING = 500000 rows it REFUSES the write rather than making room by forgetting the beginning of every journey. `by` is required and an empty one is refused. Re-stamping a reel at the station it is already at writes NOTHING and reports `wrote: False, ok: True` — but a genuine re-entry (PRINTER -> TRIAGE -> PRINTER) is a different fact and is kept.
- **river_vocab.py** — Declares which QUESTION each of the four station/stage lists answers — position (reel_router), work ladder (river), printer-internal, progress (reel_story) — and flags journal stamps no module declared.
      ↪ questions · stations_in_journal · undeclared · health
      ⚠ It unifies nothing and renames nothing — each module KEEPS its list; the fix was saying which question each answers, because position and owed work are genuinely different (two reels at the same station can owe different gates, and conflating them is how `route` became the retention tag). It IMPORTS reel_router.STATIONS rather than copying it, since a second copy of the river is the exact defect it exists to end. The gate refuses a FIFTH list that answers none of the four questions. The journal's `UNKNOWN` stamp was declared by no module at all.
- **river_walk.py** — Follows ONE named reel — its stage, what holds it, its station and gate — and adds the one thing nothing else publishes: the map from a GATE to the LANE that would open it.
      ↪ walk · main
      ⚠ It WALKS and never DRIVES, and cannot be made to: all five lane ticks (retro_triage, vault_autoreel, chronicle_autoread, chronicle_autoreel, shadow_watch) take NO argument and each chooses its own reel, so a 'driver' would move whichever reel each tick felt like while printing the named one's stage. `stage`/`hold` (retention: why keep the bytes) and `station` (the reel's own evidence: where in the river) are two different questions and may legitimately disagree. Despite the docstring's `cannot()`, `cannot` is a LIST on the output dict, not a function.
- **unknown_shelf.py** — #77 - the ONE place the river's readers say 'UNKNOWN, not an empty shelf', so a reason passed up five layers says it once.
      ↪ not_an_empty_shelf · says_unknown
      ⚠ Leads with 'UNKNOWN, not an empty shelf — ' only when the reason does not already carry that PHRASE - never the bare word UNKNOWN, which a lock or the tombstone ledger says about something else (REG-1512). Five readers (reel_river, per_reel_routes, one_funnel, printer, reel_router) nest, and each used to prefix it, so one reason read three times. An empty reason becomes the default sentence, never an empty one.

### routines-infra (33)

- **_ascii_clean_ps1.py** — One-shot rewriter that strips non-ASCII from start_tvd_win.ps1 and install-tvd.ps1 and re-writes them BOM-first for PowerShell 5.1.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ ascii_clean · main
      ⚠ It is not a checker — running it MUTATES the two .ps1 files in place (write_bytes of BOM + cleaned text), and the two filenames are hardcoded inside main(); nothing in the repo imports or calls it.
- **auto_scope.py** — Declares what each automatic lane MAY touch, NEVER touches and how often it wakes — and checks every claim against the lane's own source.
      ↪ report · say · check_declarations · undeclared_reach_abilities · unverified_reach
      ⚠ It REPORTS scope and decides nothing — it cannot arm, stop or change a lane. `forbids` is the load-bearing half of each LANE entry: only those words are enforced against the function's reachable source, so a prose-only claim like 'never deletes' is worthless unless the forbidden word is listed.
- **blueprint.py** — Generates BLUEPRINT.md from the tree itself — capture doors, lanes, ledgers, readers, wilson lanes, rulings, the gate index, the river and the printer stream.
      ↪ render · main · gate_index · river · code_only
      ⚠ `--check` REFUSES and never regenerates — the pre-push hook grades the WORKING TREE, so a hook that rewrote BLUEPRINT.md would dirty the tree it is grading. The comparison runs through code_only() to exclude the `generated <time>` stamp, without which the gate would be red every minute and read as furniture.
- **board_sync.py** — Derives the task board from TASKS.md + git log — PENDING / IN PROGRESS / YOUR CALL / BLOCKED / COMPLETED with real commit author-dates — emitting adds and updates only; undecided rows are ORPHANED, never pruned.
      ↪ build() · parse_tasks(text) · coverage(text, rows)
- **bump_version.py** — Writes one vNNNN into all four stamps at once - bible.html D2R_BUILD.id, tv_diablo.VERSION, WINDOWS_SHIP.json ver, control_app /api/status ver - verifying each; refuses apostrophes and var(--x).
      ↪ python3 tv/bump_version.py v<N> <short name> <note...> · bump()
      ⚠ The note lands in a SINGLE-QUOTED JS literal inside bible.html, so an apostrophe (v1478 blanked the 37k-line page) or a callable CSS token `var(--x)` is refused rather than escaped. Versions must be whole and carry the leading v: a bare `1613` writes four stamps that agree and no reader can parse.
- **ci_sim.py** — Runs the suite with the host things a GitHub runner lacks (his recorded board, his footage) stubbed and names which tests depend on his Mac; green means 'no KNOWN host trap', never 'CI will pass'.
      ↪ python3 tv/ci_sim.py · apply_stubs(verbose=True)
- **code_staleness.py** — The stale-image watchdog: records each watched module's (mtime,size) at first use and answers FRESH / STALE / UNKNOWN about whether this process still runs the code on disk.
      ↪ snapshot · state · report · registry_may_be_incomplete
      ⚠ It CHANGES and BLOCKS nothing — no reload, no restart, no button. And a module that never called snapshot() answers UNKNOWN, not FRESH: 'nothing snapshotted' is not the same as current. snapshot() is a one-shot no-op on re-call by design — re-snapping would adopt today's disk as the baseline and the watchdog could never report STALE again. Its real purpose is `registry_may_be_incomplete`: it separates 'I do not know this evidence source' from 'this source may not exist', the conflation that made a 16-hour-old console accuse its own .self_arming.jsonl of forgery.
- **conftest.py** — Session-wide pytest canaries — fails the run if a test changed the bytes of gitignored live data or left a child process running.
      ↪ live_data_is_not_collateral · no_orphaned_children · redirect_path
      ⚠ It is a CANARY, not a lock — it reports damage after the bytes are gone. The isolation API is `redirect_module_path` (patch the module ATTRIBUTE and assert it took); `os.environ[...]` inside a test is a no-op for constants bound at import, which is how chron_evidence.json went 525,187 -> 748 bytes.
- **console_keepalive.py** — REG-2614 - the Windows console watchdog. Task Scheduler runs it every 5 min (task 'TV DIABLO keepalive', pythonw, hidden, as the signed-in user); a pass connects to :17772 and starts start_tvd_win.ps1 -Background only when the port REFUSES.
      ↪ run_once · decide · probe · main
      ⚠ Slow (a timed-out connect) and unknown are left alone. tv/.tvd_exited_on_purpose (written by control_app's exit surface, cleared at boot) and tv/.tvd_supervisor_pause stop it; one launch per 10 min, booked in tv/.tvd_keepalive.json before it is tried. Stdlib only, imports nothing of the console's. The task is made and withdrawn by console_doctor.ensure_keepalive_task on the sign-in start's consent.
- **console_safe.py** — enable() rewraps stdout/stderr with errors='replace' so a cp1255 console cannot turn a passing gate's own verdict print into exit 1.
      ↪ enable
      ⚠ It is the single most-imported module here — 457 files call it — and it only works if called FIRST, before anything prints. Three separate regressions (REG-044, REG-054, REG-077) were correct code reporting FAILURE because the success branch died inside print('✅ …'). Any new tv/*.py entry point that prints non-ASCII needs the line; nothing enforces that automatically.
- **disk_room.py** — #151 (REG-2742) - the one free-space reading every deciding floor uses (ON AIR, picture size, disk sign, doctor, retention, warden, river, heart2 lanes), counting what macOS frees on demand, as Finder does.
      ↪ free_bytes · free_gb · important_bytes
      ⚠ On macOS it returns the larger of what CoreFoundation says an IMPORTANT write may use (purgeable space included) and the plain free; anywhere else, or when the key will not answer, the plain free. A law that replaced shutil.disk_usage gets the plain value of ITS pretend disk - judged from the function itself (module 'shutil', name 'disk_usage'), never from a copy taken at import, because this module is imported late. It raises exactly what shutil.disk_usage raises, so each caller keeps its own UNKNOWN handling.
- **fixture_ledgers.py** — redirect() points TV_UNKNOWN_AGE and TV_EAGLE_SLOW at pid-scoped temp files for a TEST process, so suites that drive console_doctor / unknown_age never write his live eagle ledgers.
      ↪ redirect
      ⚠ For test processes ONLY — production code must never import it. It fills only an UNSET variable, so an explicit per-test redirect in setUp still wins. Seven suites call it at import (v3470); test_no_test_writes_his_eagle_ledgers pins that by AST, and run_gates._LIVE_STATE now names both ledgers so CI FAILS on a write.
- **fixture_tmp.py** — contain() gives a TEST process one parent scratch directory (tempfile.tempdir + TMPDIR/TEMP/TMP) removed at exit, so every mkdtemp a suite makes - paired or not - leaves with it (#171).
      ↪ contain · sweep_stale
      ⚠ For test processes ONLY - production must never import it (the law checks by AST). A KILLED run never reaches atexit, so the next contain() sweeps parents whose owner pid is dead AND that are over a day old, never a live run's. 46 suites call it at import; test_a_test_run_leaves_no_scratch_dirs finds any file with an unpaired site that does not.
- **gate_costs.py** — the MEASURED CI seconds of every gate (tv/gate_costs.json), so run_gates --shard balances the CI matrix on real cost instead of declared timeout.
      ↪ load · from_logs · main
      ⚠ A measurement with a source, committed so every machine cuts the same slices. A gate the table has not seen weighs the MEDIAN, not its declared timeout. Refresh from CI job logs with python3 tv/gate_costs.py <logs>; test_the_gate_set_shards_cleanly refuses a table covering under 95% of the registry.
- **git_quiet.py** — The ONE door every git spawn in this codebase walks through, so no git child ever owns a console.
      ↪ run
      ⚠ Git for Windows' PATH git (Git\cmd\git.exe) is a 46 KB CUI WRAPPER and pythonw owns no console, so a CUI child ALLOCATES one — a real terminal on top of D2R. MEASURED 2026-09-21: windows stealing focus 2-3 in a row every couple of minutes. CREATE_NO_WINDOW on the wrapper is NOT enough because it respawns the real git without the flag, and headless-git.exe still starts a CUI git.exe child — so this calls mingw64\bin\git.exe DIRECTLY. It is a module rather than a helper inside control_app because console_doctor spawns git too and a second copy would drift; test_windows_git_spawns_hide_the_console pins that there may only ever be ONE body of the mingw redirect.
- **grok_chat_prune.py** — #151 (REG-2743, his ruling) - prunes his own Grok CLI chats automatically when they need to be, so ~/.grok/sessions cannot take the disk to ENOSPC again (11.25 GB on 2026-09-01), with a receipt per pass.
      ↪ run · plan · inventory · live_ids · sessions_root
      ⚠ It prunes only when NEEDED (the Grok sessions folder over 2 GB, or the disk under the ON AIR floor) and never a session active_sessions.json lists as open - an unreadable registry prunes nothing.
