Whole conversation folders, oldest first, never one written in the last hour or younger than 7 days (1 under the critical floor), never anything outside the root or behind a link. The console calls it from the disk sign's pass, which already waits out his game and a busy machine.
- **handoff.py** — Drains the GrokBot/Grok handoff queues in O(new) rather than O(all), using a stored watermark plus the REST `since` parameter, and sorts each comment into ACT / ASK / FYI so a brief cannot be lost in a wall of watch ticks.
      ↪ main
      ⚠ ⚠ THE WATERMARK MOVES ONLY WITH --mark, NEVER BY DRAINING - so reading the queue can never mark an unanswered ACT as read. Measured 2026-09-18: #180 held 1,177 comments / 2.55 MB and was taking 140 comments a day against 10 replies; the watermark drain is 65.5x cheaper. It still drains the CLOSED queues 179/180 on purpose, because the GrokBot native seat kept posting there after the rotation to #230.
- **keep_awake.py** — #170 (REG-2483): keeps this PC awake ONLY while a proof or sweep runs - held(why) for a with-block on this thread, hold_for_pid(pid, why) for a process (idempotent, ends by itself when the pid ends, normally or killed), status() in the shared vocabulary {on, holds, lastTs, why}. self_prove takes a hold for the prover it spawns and for one a relaunched console adopts.
      ↪ held · hold_for_pid · status · caffeinate_argv
      ⚠ The Windows execution state is PER THREAD and the OS drops it when that thread ends, so a pid hold lives on its own watcher thread (take, WaitForSingleObject on the process handle, release with ES_CONTINUOUS alone - all on that thread); a release from any other thread releases nothing. The flags are exactly 0x80000001 - never ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED (his screen still turns off), never away mode, never powercfg or the registry. macOS is `caffeinate -i -w <pid>`, spawned with an absolute executable and close_fds=False because the console has Quartz loaded (#150's fork deadlock). A lid close still sleeps the PC: that stays the OS's call. self_prove holds a pid for real only when its OWN doors (spawn, pid_alive, proc_birth, identity - captured at import) started or verified it: a law that stubs any of them hands a stand-in pid, and a real caffeinate on that number holds whatever process owns it (the first cut started three a run from the REG-1628 chain law, one outliving the law).
- **launcher_icon.py** — On each launch, gives every TV DIABLO.app on the Desktop or in Applications that runs this repo's start_tvd_mac.sh the repo's current appicon.icns.
      ↪ candidates · refresh
      ⚠ Only the icon resource is replaced and the bundle touched; a bundle is never moved, renamed or deleted, and one that runs another tree is left alone.
- **my_orphans.py** — Reports busy, long-lived processes I started and left running (>20% CPU, >20 min) so he is never the one who notices his Mac is hot; it KILLS nothing.
      ↪ suspects · holds_his_port · main
      ⚠ holds_his_port() is the whole point of the file and DID NOT EXIST for a long time: HIS_PORTS (17772, 17781, 17955, 9222, 9223, 8848) was declared with a comment claiming it protected his console and `grep -c HIS_PORTS` returned 1 — its own definition. It FAILS CLOSED: if lsof cannot answer, the port is UNKNOWN and the process is NOT-MINE. On Windows there is no lsof, so EVERY process fails closed there (none is ever counted as mine, and the spawn ledger and the tree are never asked) - the orphans row says exactly that instead of claiming the three witnesses (REG-2482). KNOWN is not a convenience list — it is what keeps the sweep from becoming furniture, and D2R.exe/CrossOver/wineserver are in it because HIS GAME IS NOT AN ORPHAN (334% CPU for 100 minutes while he played).
- **node_runtime.py** — #167 (REG-2484): the ONE door that puts Node.js on a Windows PC where it is missing - for tv/install-tvd.ps1 (`--ensure`, exactly one call, after the clone) and for the console (ensure(blocking=False)) - so the node-only laws and the tooltip-oracle lane run on every PC, not only where an agent placed node by hand. It re-resolves cb_node_harness.NODE after placing it, so no relaunch is needed.
      ↪ ensure · load_pin · target_dir · main
      ⚠ Fetches ONLY on Windows (macOS and Linux resolve and say so), and only the zip tv/node_pin.json pins for this PC's architecture: its SHA-256 is checked BEFORE anything is extracted, and the extracted node.exe against the pin's own exe hash, before the rename into %LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\nodejs. PATH changes for THIS process only (os.environ) - no setx, no registry, no profile, no admin; its children (the prover) inherit it. blocking=False fetches on ONE background thread per process and answers ok None while it runs; a failed fetch is said and not retried in a loop. It never spawns a process. A new node version is a new pin: copy every hash from that version's SHASUMS256.txt.
- **one_name.py** — The one identity table: any spelling (set/sets, unique/uniques/uni, runeword/rw) → concept, and concept+surface → the exact form template / route / lane / ledger each keys on. Flattens nothing.
      ↪ concept(name) · form(name, surface) · same_thing(a, b)
- **replay.py** — Replays a journaled session's archived frames (tv/frames/hist + the sessions.jsonl rotation ring) through the real tv_diablo --watch loop under TV_STUB with TV_OCR=0 — recorded truth, no vision spend.
      ↪ tvd replay [--list | --n N | --pace S] · load_journal() · build_manifest(reads, frames_dir)
- **routine_i_shards.py** — Deals Routine I's fast Playwright specs onto N shards by measured duration, largest first onto the lightest shard, so one heavy file is not the whole run's floor.
      ↪ fast_specs · deal · file_weight · load_costs · main
      ⚠ The table tv/routine_i_costs.json is one run's measurement (per-file sum of every attempt). A file it has not seen weighs the median, never zero. An unreadable table still names every fast spec and says the balance is unmeasured. The slow sims stay out of this list; their names are the chromium project's testIgnore, read from playwright.config.ts. An empty shard exits 2, because Playwright with no paths runs the whole project.
- **safe_copy.py** — Copies the repo to scratch OUTSIDE it, minus frames/.git/.render_shots, and REFUSES past a size ceiling rather than filling his disk.
      ↪ copy · plan
      ⚠ It is NOT a general copier and must not become one — .git and frames/ are in HEAVY, so the copy has no history and no footage, which is fine for a sabotage test and wrong for anything else. It also refuses a destination inside the repo (the next walk would copy the copy) and refuses over MAX_MB before writing a byte. It exists because three agents each ran `cp -R tv /tmp/...` and wrote 20.5 GB in four minutes onto a volume with 9 GB free, hitting ENOSPC.
- **simulate.py** — Scripted fake session through tv_diablo's real bridge — timed reads plus a simulated heartbeat — so the bible's TV receiver (connect, feed, routing, apply, offline) can be debugged with no game.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ python3 tv/simulate.py [--fast] · main()
- **soak_test.py** — The 30-minute night-run soak: boots tv_diablo.py in TV_STUB mode and asserts the process survives, RSS stays bounded, the journal stays line-valid JSON, and posted intake beats are not LOST.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ rss_mb · post_intake
      ⚠ It is a top-level SCRIPT, not a unittest — nothing imports it; CI runs it via tv-soak.yml. Run bare on his Mac it pins a process for THIRTY minutes (TV_SOAK_MINUTES default 30) and binds port 17958; use TV_SOAK_MINUTES=2 locally. It strips ANTHROPIC_API_KEY from the child env so the soak can never spend. The RSS leak rule is relative AND absolute (late > early×1.8 AND +300MB) and needs ≥8 samples, so a short soak asserts nothing about memory.
- **space_warden.py** — Reclaims only REGENERABLE output when free space drops under FLOOR_GB, re-verifying each path is inside the repo, git-ignored and untracked; proposes the rest.
      ↪ survey · reclaim · main
      ⚠ It is an OWNER, not a policy — the 9.5 GB Playwright blowout happened because test-results/ had no owner, while three pruners each owned something else. It auto-deletes ONLY what a named command can regenerate; tv/frames/hist and tv/frames/corpus are listed as `propose` and deleted by nobody (reel_retention owns that and needs a yes). Above FLOOR_GB (default 20, TV_SPACE_FLOOR_GB) it does nothing at all unless forced, and `reclaim()` defaults to dry_run=True.
- **stamp_versions.py** — Backfills TASKS.md's version table, binding each vNNNN to the commit whose diff ADDED `VERSION = "vNNNN"` to tv/tv_diablo.py.
      ↪ stamp · version_commits · resolve · table_region · row_cells
      ⚠ The authority is the VERSION STAMP, never the commit subject — a subject can mention a version it does not ship. And there are THREE answers, not two: bound (230), CARRIED (19 versions batched into a later commit, which get their own word rather than being called bound or unknown), and unknown. Its row regex must tolerate the annotation it writes itself, or the audit undercounts its own output.
- **tree_busy.py** — Answers ONE question with ONE definition: is this working tree being graded right now? `hooks/pre-push` grades the WORKING TREE rather than the commit, so a version banked mid-run gets a green verdict about bytes that are not the ones shipping. bump_version consults it and REFUSES to stamp when the answer is anything but free. It returns a REASON string rather than a bare bool, so the refusal can say which signal fired.
      ↪ why · main
      ⚠ ⚠⚠ TWO SIGNALS, AND THE LOCK ALONE IS NOT ENOUGH. run_gates takes the flock, but the hook runs renders, console demos and the Playwright smoke OUTSIDE that claim - MEASURED during the v3328 push, the flock read FREE while a pre-push had been grading 15 minutes with smoke in flight. ⚠ UNKNOWN IS NOT FREE, and EITHER signal going dark is enough: the first cut used `and` so a blind flock plus a clean process list fell through to free with the primary signal silent - the second eye found it, and the law could not have, because it stubbed both signals as None. ⚠ The process probe matches an INVOCATION, never a MENTION: `pgrep -f hooks/pre-push` also matches `vi hooks/pre-push`, which would refuse every bump while an editor stayed open. ⚠ It RELEASES the flock the moment it has tested it - holding it would make the checker the second gate run REG-162 is about. ⚠ Its reach is bounded and stated: an ad-hoc heredoc that writes bible.html directly will never consult a module it does not import.
- **win_quiet.py** — The one door that makes every child of a Windows console/agent process windowless (REG-1307).
      ↪ install · installed
      ⚠ pythonw.exe owns no console, so EVERY console-subsystem child (git, powershell, schtasks, tasklist, python.exe) allocates a visible window that steals focus from a full-screen game unless the spawn passes CREATE_NO_WINDOW. MEASURED 2026-09-26: 82 spawn sites in 38 tv/ modules passed no creationflags. This replaces subprocess.Popen ONCE per process (run/call/check_output build Popen from the module's globals), so it must be installed BEFORE the first spawn - control_app and tv_diablo do it at import. A deliberate CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE / DETACHED_PROCESS is kept. git_quiet.py still owns git (its wrapper respawns the real git, so the binary is called directly).
- **windows_boot_check.py** — Boots a SCRATCH console (TV_STUB=1, TV_CAPTURE=off, own port and sandbox) and asks /api/status which build it runs, against tv/WINDOWS_SHIP.json. The Windows CI job (tv-windows-boot.yml) runs it on windows-latest (#229).  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ main
      ⚠ Exit 77 = could not run (no manifest), never a pass. TVD_BOOT_CHECK_TREE points it at another tree, which is how it was proven on the ALT over SSH without touching his checkout. The console is stopped by its own PID and the sandbox removed whatever happens.
- **windows_keepalive_check.py** — REG-2614 - on windows-latest (called by windows_boot_check.py after its boot passes) registers the keepalive task XML under a scratch name with the real Task Scheduler, reads it back, deletes it, and drives one keepalive pass against a closed port with a recording launcher.
      ↪ main
      ⚠ Exit 77 off Windows. Never launches anything for real; the scratch task is deleted in a finally.

### routing (2)

- **route_lane_wilson.py** — Thirty-one distinct mutations of each route lane's OWN predicate — chronicle _source_ok, fleet _defines, roster _declared — counting which ones the predicate still accepts.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ main
      ⚠ It attacks the predicates routes() actually calls, so a mutation is one idea about one guard rather than another copy of 'delete the file'. A mutation the predicate still accepts is a LEAK and is not banked. Nothing here edits his bible or his rosters — chronicle's check is pointed at a temp file. Written on the Windows box during the 2026-09-22 hardening arc.
- **trace_spine.py** — Follows one named item across reel, ledger, routing decision and endpoint, and proves the negative half: that a chronicle or farming scenario deposits nothing in the vault.
      ↪ name_key · independence · hop_reel · hop_ledger · scenario_of · may_vault · hop_routing · hop_endpoint · spine · negative · main
      ⚠ Follows ONE named item across reel -> ledger -> routing -> endpoint, and asserts the NEGATIVE half too: a chronicle or farming scenario must produce ZERO vault rows. ⚠ It REPORTS and never writes — footage has no un-delete and the witnessed machinery stays the only writer.

### second-eye (11)

- **coldread.py** — Stages a shipped bible.html AT THE REPO ROOT and screenshots tabs/widths/regions for the cold second-eye pass, refusing to hand over a bad frame.
      ↪ main · region_absence_verdict
      ⚠ The staged copy MUST live at the repo root (`.coldread.tmp.html`): `git show` into a scratch dir makes every relative `art/…` src 404 — measured 50 broken images vs 5 — and the eye then reports a regression that does not exist. A tab switch is a 0.28s fade, so it waits for full opacity and measures it; a black capture, an unsettled panel or an over-baseline broken-image count STOPS the run (exit 2), because a look recorded through a broken instrument is worse than no look.
- **disk_report_crossfamily.py** — Runs the cold attacks a non-Anthropic family designed on credible_pruned_mb (-0.0, 0.9 MB against an empty corpus, 2 MB against 1 MiB) as prune.reports' second, independent evidence kind.
      ↪ prove() · bank_into_proof_queue(rep) · python3 tv/disk_report_crossfamily.py --bank
- **eye_vs_beat.py** — Stamps the console's live panel beat onto each human-eyes observation and flags the one mechanical contradiction — the eye saw a blank screen while the beat claims a panel shown with height > 0.
      ↪ report() · judge(row) · capture_beat() · python3 tv/eye_vs_beat.py --json
- **g5_grok_eyes.py** — The second eye's vision lane: `grok -p` on his SuperGrok login, off/shadow/primary, reading the same area/tz/scene/names/names_loc/sockets JSON under an hourly+daily budget.
      ↪ g5_vision_read() · g5_chronicle_read() · mode()/set_mode()/is_primary() · status() · g5_shadow_log()
      ⚠ API-style secrets are stripped from the child env on purpose (_API_STRIP) — this is the subscription CLI lane, never api.x.ai; OFF is an instant no-op returning None, and chronicle_doctor treats an unimportable g5 as 'the second eye is unavailable', never as agreement.
- **g5_shadow_reducer.py** — Reduces tv/.g5_shadow.jsonl - the 6,082 Claude-vs-Grok rows written since 2026-08-23 that nothing had ever read - into an agreement report carrying its own denominator and window.
      ↪ reduce_rows · main
      ⚠ READ-SIDE ONLY. It never writes the shadow log and never prefers a lane: a 67.6% disagreement rate is a finding about the INSTRUMENTS, not a vote to settle. One-sided rows (only one lane answered) are NOT disagreements and are counted separately; rows where neither answered are not evidence at all. A rate with no denominator is UNKNOWN, never 0.0.
- **human_eyes_ledger.py** — Records the Grok-Bot human-eyes loop: which briefs were SENT, which came back with a raw OBSERVATION plus verdict, and which are still owed.
      ↪ send · observed · state · owed · proven
      ⚠ All three of its file-IO handlers return None, never [] — swallow_census cites this file as the purest instance of the honest shape, because None lets a caller tell 'could not ask' from 'no briefs'; flipping them to [] is the RANK-1 defect the ratchet exists to stop. It stores `saw` (raw observation) separately from `conclusion` on purpose: a brief that returns a conclusion with no observation reads like evidence and is not — a Grok pass quoted PROJECT_VAULT_MANAGER.md accurately and reported the wrong gate numbers. The ledger (.human_eyes.jsonl, overridable via TV_HUMAN_EYES_LEDGER) is a runtime record and is gitignored.
- **pixel_witness_crossfamily.py** — A cross-family (different model/eye) attack set against paint_witness.verdict, banked under src `pixel_witness_crossfamily` for the console.pixel_rescue lock.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ attacks · run · main
      ⚠ refused=True means the witness did NOT fall — the attack was REFUSED, which is the good outcome; reading it as a failure inverts the whole score. Its value is that the attacks were designed OUTSIDE pixel_witness_wilson's own sabotage list, so banking it under the same src as the same-family attacks would destroy the independence that is its only reason to exist.
- **second_eye_drain.py** — Drains the #231 SECOND-EYE lane into tv/.second_eye.jsonl, so a cross-family look that actually happened becomes a look the pre-push gate can SEE. The seat posts version / sha / verdict / model / chars / reach / findings to GitHub and nothing carried it to the ledger, so the gate asking "was this version looked at" answered OWED about looks that existed.
      ↪ main
      ⚠ ⚠⚠ IT COPIES, IT NEVER JUDGES - a drainer that re-scored a verdict would be a second opinion wearing the first one's name. It also NEVER moves handoff's watermark (one owner per watermark, or a human drain of #231 would silently skip whatever this consumed) and NEVER invents a family (`model` goes in verbatim and family_of() decides). Idempotent through verdictFrom "gh#231 comment <id>", read back from the LEDGER itself rather than a side file - a side watermark drifts and then a re-drain doubles a look, inflating `looks` and turning one witness into a false corroboration. A look saying `version: unknown` is REFUSED and COUNTED: filing it would mint a version called "unknown" that every stampless look piles into. MEASURED 2026-09-23, and it cost a whole review: the #231 seat reviewed v3449 at 181,141 chars and named BOTH defects later rediscovered by a fresh Grok call and shipped as v3452 - it sat unread because nothing carried GitHub looks to the ledger.
- **second_eye_ledger.py** — Which shipped versions the second eye actually LOOKED at - model, family, images and findings per version; hooks/pre-push reads it, and no entry is a refusal rather than a pass.
      ↪ record() · looked_at() · owes_a_look() · audit() · --check <ver> --gate
      ⚠ `family` is DERIVED from the model id, not asserted — a same-family agent writing plausible strings must not read as a cross-family look; reached=False is recorded, kept, and does NOT satisfy the gate; the .second_eye.jsonl record is untracked runtime state.
- **second_eye_run.py** — Runs a cold cross-family review of a shipped version end to end — finds the commit, strips the diff's comments, MEASURES the payload it is about to send, asks, and records the answer into second_eye_ledger.
      ↪ run_one · payload_for · ask · record_answer · main
      ⚠ `sentCode` must be measured from the PROMPT TEXT by this script — hand-filling it put a typed 7,250 into an evidence ledger whose real value was 7,366, because code_was_transmitted() handed a dict returns the honest {chars:0}. An unreachable eye is recorded reached=False with NO verdict, never silence-as-agreement. COLD means cold: the framing says nothing about what changed or what anyone suspects, and the diff's own comments are stripped because this repo's comments name the defect and its fix.
- **vault_apply_crossfamily.py** — Runs the cold attacks a non-Anthropic family designed against vault.apply's write door (an uncorroborated row under `unsure` bypassing the re-gate; owned=None) and banks them as the cross-family kind.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ prove() · bank_into_proof_queue(rep) · python3 tv/vault_apply_crossfamily.py

### shadow (3)

- **farmed_names.py** — Snaps OCR words from a session to known D2 item names (item_tables.json) and groups what a run farmed, with counts.
      ↪ snap · clean · group · table · vault_denylist
      ⚠ A word that matches nothing is dropped, never shown; UI and NPC words come from bible.html _NOT_ITEMS plus SESSION_UI_WORDS; potions and other consumables are never farmed, uber keys and organs are.
- **session_route.py** — v3859 (#230) - the session route ledger: walks one session's reads and files every item as farmed, identified, stashed, trashed, carried or seen, the one route the owner Sessions tab and every shadow read.
      ↪ route · place · chronicle_eligible
      ⚠ A floor-only sighting is seen, never farmed; an item read from a trade, vendor, gamble or shared-stash screen is carried, never farmed. Identify renames a farmed item without counting it again. A chronicle find needs the green 'eligible for chronicle' line.
- **shadow_sessions.py** — #218 phase 1 (his ruling 2026-10-28: a shadow renders the owner's data one to one, with summarized key frames - full reels never leave their PC): the owner's newest 16 sessions, dated by what they captured, read counts, names read, and up to 3 key-frame thumbnails each, under a 600 KB cap.
      ↪ digest · thumb_bytes
      ⚠ Reads are counted by the Shelf's own predicate (control_app._is_a_live_read, passed in) - never a copy of it. A frame rides only as the theatre's 160 px thumbnail (cache160); a missing one is made by sips on a Mac, and a PC with no resizer carries the session without pictures and says why - never a full frame. The cap drops the OLDEST sessions' pictures first.

### shelf-journal (3)

- **journal_retention.py** — Classifies shelf JOURNAL rows as RELEASABLE (footageState == retired) or HELD, keeps the newest 8, and explains every hold. Writes nothing.
      ↪ plan · main
      ⚠ Name suggests the reel river; it is the OTHER half. The reel side is finished (20 reels, ROUTED 20, TOMBSTONE 0 — correct, all three refusal groups are legitimate) while his shelf still shows 419 journal rows, ~195 of which name film that no longer exists. 'No film at all' is HELD, not extracted — absence of film is not proof of extraction. And it DELETES NOTHING; the apply half is his decision. Four rows are never releasable: newest 8, film still on disk, reels the test suite opens by name, and undatable rows.
- **shelf_corroborate.py** — Compares three independent witnesses to one reel's frame count — dossier, shelf card, f_*.jpg counted fresh on disk — and reports where they disagree.
      ↪ corroborate · witnesses · report · scene_witnesses
      ⚠ The dossier is the odd witness every time (52 of 53 reels on first run): control_app._theatre_sessions counts JOURNAL ROWS in the group, not film, and replay.split_sessions then divides those rows across duplicate groups — a label naming another quantity, split by a second bug. It REPORTS and never repairs, because making two witnesses agree by editing one buries the contradiction.
- **shelf_driver.py** — Runs what reel_retention.plan() decides for the shelf: owed/held/releasable lanes, river stages, lane census and its own heartbeat.
      ↪ work · stages · plan · lane_census · beat
      ⚠ It holds NO second predicate on purpose — it carries retention's own tag and why through untouched, because every serious 2026-09-10 defect was two authorities answering one question (41 vs 0, 40 vs 0, 2 vs 19). It never deletes: _PRUNE_SAFE_TO_RUN is Konyo's to arm. _beat_path() resolves at CALL time via tv_diablo._fixture_root so TV_HIST can redirect it, and REFUSES (returns None, beat records it could not persist) rather than writing into his live tv/. work() returns ok False with owed=None on a failed plan — an empty list is a measurement, never a failure.

### testing (1)

- **scenario_frames.py** — #22 (his rulings 2026-10-28): each TESTING_PHASE hand-test scenario joined to its items (roster or read names only), each item to the frames of his whose reads named it, each frame to its own picture, and REC-03 to its recorded check. A report: reads the journals, retro_triage.json and TESTING_PHASE.md; writes nothing.
      ↪ report · load_frames · scenario_ids · scenario_items · check_rec03 · main
      ⚠ A read's picture is <frameId>.jpg at the reel root; the reel folder's f_<ms>.jpg are FOOTAGE stills whose ms need not match a read's capture time (the first cut joined to them and found 0 of every sample on disk). A base read beside a runeword is that runeword's base, never a white-base control. A KAI/watchdog note is not a read. Character panels are labelled nowhere until #36 builds that reader.

### unknown (2)

- **claude_signin.py** — REG-1617: the SIGN IN button under the CLAUDE/GROK lamps. status() asks `claude auth status --json` for the CLI's own loggedIn; start() opens `claude auth login` in a window on THIS PC (Windows: a visible console; Mac: Terminal) and he finishes in the browser. control_app.claude_login() is the origin-guarded route, _claude_auth_state() the cached background probe that lets the lamp say 'signed out' before a read has to fail.
      ↪ status · command · start · inflight
      ⚠ The console hides every child it starts on Windows (win_quiet, REG-1307), and win_quiet still hides a CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE child unless the caller hands in its own STARTUPINFO - so start() passes both, with SW_SHOWNORMAL: a sign-in he pressed for must be SEEN, or a prompt in it hangs for ever. status() None is UNKNOWN (no CLI, no answer, garbage), never 'signed out'. One sign-in in flight at a time; the command is fixed and nothing from the page reaches it.
- **seed_worktree.py** — Seeds a fresh push worktree with the gitignored state its pre-push gates read (node_modules, tv/.heart2.json, a per-checkout second-eye ledger only where one is still kept), finding the main checkout from the worktree's own .git pointer (#143, REG-2451).
      ↪ seed · main_checkout_of · main
      ⚠ It copies, never links, and never overwrites: an item already in the worktree is left as it is, and it refuses (exit 2, nothing copied) on the main checkout itself, a path that is not a worktree root, or a seed that would leave free disk under the ON AIR floor (TV_SEED_FREE_BYTES is the law's seam for the free-space reading).

### vault (28)

- **affix_lexicon.py** — Generates tv/affix_lexicon.json from the D2R CASC and classifies an item NAME against it. The roster names uniques, sets and runewords; MAGIC and RARE names are COMPOSED at drop time from four affix tables plus a base type, so they can never be a list. Measured off the install: magicPrefix 269, magicSuffix 298, rarePrefix 42, rareSuffix 152, baseType 690. classify() answers GRAIL / BASE / MAGIC / RARE / UNKNOWN and vault_retro attaches it to every unsure row as `vocab`.
      ↪ build · load · classify · verify · main
      ⚠ THE house `perl -e 'alarm N; exec @ARGV'` TIMEOUT IDIOM KILLS THE EXTRACTOR. /usr/bin/perl is SIP-restricted so dyld strips DYLD_* across that exec and casc_extract dies with 'Library not loaded: @rpath/casc.framework' (rc -6) - measured both ways on one file, 59,837 bytes direct vs 0 wrapped. The env is built INSIDE the subprocess call and subprocess carries its own timeout. ALSO: item-names.json is NOT a base-type list - it is the full 1,593-name string table with uniques in it, so baseType comes from armor/weapons/misc instead. AND classify() never returns a best PARTIAL: an unmatched token or two parses of differing kind is UNKNOWN, because a wrong kind becomes a wrong routing decision about his loot.
- **char_builder_db.py** — #174 v-B2: generates the Character Builder's item database from his install's CASC - every base (type, tier family, sockets, requirements, defense / damage, hands, size, whether it can be ethereal), every unique, set item, runeword (with the item types it may be made in and those it excludes) and crafted cube recipe, each with its property lines rendered from the game's own strings and every rolled value a range; the runes and gems that fill a socket; the classes' skill-tab strings; and per doll slot the rail of base categories (itemtypes.txt UICategory + class locks). The doctor row 'builder item data' runs its --check (#174 v-B2 fix round). A per-level line is ["L", lo, key, hi, shift] (the par, or the min..max roll, and itemstatcost's op param), a random class ["C", lo, key, hi].
      ↪ build · render · embedded · check · write · main
      ⚠ It WRITES bible.html (atomic, refuses when bible.html.EDIT_LOCK is held) between the two ⟦CB_DB⟧ markers and nowhere else, as a <script type="application/json"> the builder parses on first open. Every property line is rendered from the game's own words (properties.txt -> itemstatcost.txt descfunc / descstrpos -> item-modifiers.json), and each rolled value keeps the COLUMN it came from (p3 = prop3 / T1Code3 / mod 3, a2b = a set item's aprop2b) - the key the builder stores a typed roll under. A property the tables name but do not describe becomes an UNKNOWN line naming its code, never dropped. Without an install --check is 77 = UNKNOWN; CI checks the block's names against tv/item_tables.json instead. The duplicate display names are the game's (8 Rainbow Facets, the Colossal Jewel printing "Jewel"): they are told apart by the property that differs, or by the table name - never by position.
- **char_props.py** — #174 v-B2: generates the character sheet's property data from his install - uniqueitems / setitems / sets / runes / gems prop lines with par-min-max, properties.txt code -> (func, stat, val), itemstatcost per-level shifts and description strings, difficultylevels ResistPenalty (0 / -40 / -100), the base tables' defense / sockets / level, the class and skill-tab strings - into one CHAR_PROPS block that window.D2R_CHAR_ENGINE.sheet() sums. The doctor row 'character sheet data' runs its --check.
      ↪ assemble · build · render · embedded · check · write · main
      ⚠ It WRITES bible.html (atomic, refuses when bible.html.EDIT_LOCK is held) between the CHAR_PROPS:BEGIN / CHAR_PROPS:END markers inside the v174-char-engine-js script and nowhere else. --check without an install is 77 = UNKNOWN, never green; it compares the WHOLE derived block, so a hand edit under an unchanged sourceHash is still STALE. Anya's +10 resistance scroll is in no table - the engine applies it as a named rule. Never wrap the pull in the perl alarm idiom (see affix_lexicon).
- **d2jsp_chrome.py** — Posts one priced D2JSP draft to board 268 (271 on a ladder console) through the Chrome login already on this machine, one send at a time and never twice, and reads a Switch Ladder fg range the same way, only from lines that name the item.
      ↪ d2jsp_send · d2jsp_quote · parse_public_quote · parse_compose · cookie_header_from_rows · decrypt_v10 · seal_v10 · chrome_user_agent · console_may_post · SEND_REASONS · d2jsp_login_status · open_d2jsp_login · _sweep_stale_scratch
      ⚠ A post is submitted only when the board shows the new topic: the run lands on topic.php, or the board list read again after the click holds a topic whose text is exactly the subject, that was not listed before the click and is newer than every topic listed then (an older same-subject topic is never the receipt). A click with no receipt is maybe-sent with the board and the time, never 'nothing was posted'; only a run that never clicked, or one the board sent the compose form back to, is not-posted. Reaching the form and the receipt have separate budgets (25 s, then 35 s). One send runs at a time: a second is busy and reads no cookie. The same draft (board, subject, body) is not sent again for 30 minutes after a submit (already-posted) or a maybe-sent (maybe-sent-recently), unless a later send changed that topic: a send drops every other draft's record on the topic it changed or made, and an edit is held back only by a record of its own topic (A, B, A edits again). That record lives in this process, so a console restart forgets it. Every answer's reason is one of SEND_REASONS, and the decisions are _flow_step, a pure function over page snapshots that a law drives through the real _Page over a fake DevTools socket. The fill script marks the page before its click, and the snapshot reads the mark back: a document without it is new, which is how the compose page a slow submit has not left yet differs from the form sent back. A DevTools error in place of a script's result (the submit's navigation tearing the page down) is no answer, never an empty one, so a fill answered that way counts as clicked. A Chrome that cannot start (no websocket module, no Chrome, no DevTools in 12 s) is chrome-start with a why. A price read never opens a window: open_quote_login_once opens nothing, and the lamp and a Post click are the only doors to the login. The cookie database is read into memory, never copied to disk. _sweep_stale_scratch removes scratch profiles and d2jsp-cookies-* copies 5 minutes stale, kills only a process naming that exact --user-data-dir (the pgrep pattern goes after --), counts only what is gone, and runs before each scratch Chrome, on every room login check and at console boot; an atexit reaper ends a scratch Chrome in flight on a normal exit (os.execv and os._exit skip it). A 200 page, a missing login, and a cookie that will not open are status words, never a claimed send. A price is the middle of the fg numbers on lines that name the item in the Switch Ladder search, board 243. A login wall, a challenge page, and a page with no such line stay unknown. The cookie header is built only from hosts that are d2jsp.org. It is not stored, logged, or put in the response. The keychain is read only when a quote or a post runs, and only on the Mac Chrome store. Windows and a page that is not this console return without posting or pricing. The desk joins the post at POST /api/d2jsp_send and the reading at GET /api/d2jsp_quote. A Chrome cookie from database 24 opens only when its first 32 bytes are SHA256 of that row host. Any other digest stays unread. A plain older value still opens. The Switch Ladder search is the board form at search.php?c=7&f=243, field stext. A challenge page is read in a scratch Chrome on its own profile. His open Chrome is not attached. A challenge that still has no fg stays unknown.
- **d2s_read.py** — Reads a D2R character save (.d2s, format 105) byte-exact: header (name, class, level, last played), the stats block by name, the 30 skill bytes, every item with location/slot/panel/x/y, code, name, quality, unique/set/runeword names, sockets and socketed children, ethereal, ilvl, defense, durability and properties as [{stat, param, value}], plus merc/golem presence and a verify block (bytesConsumed, endsOnCorpseTag, itemCount, stoppedAt).
      ↪ read · main
      ⚠ A PARTIAL ITEM LIST IS NEVER COMPLETE: the bitstream has no per-item length, so ok is True only when the declared TOP-LEVEL count (socketed children ride inside their parent) ends exactly on the corpse 'JM', the stats block ends on 'if', and the file's size and checksum hold. Format 105 adds ONE bit after the type-specific block and ONE bit after the last property list before byte-align - the study decoder's '8 bits after a runeword list' and sniffed '0x00 pad' are that same trailing bit on a list that ends on a byte boundary. A runeword past runes.txt row 79 is named None with both candidates, because row and key disagree there and no save has told which the game means.
- **equipped_ledger.py** — #54 - what each CHARACTER wears, per doll slot, with the slot's box in that frame's pixels, filed at every seal from the journal's names_loc-equipped reads, keyed by the login (char-select) row and carried across the hourly shadow reels of one game session.
      ↪ ingest(journals,hist_dir) : · contract(journals) : · game_sessions(reels) : · worn_from_row(row,hist_dir) : · slot_box(slot,w,h) : · character_of_row(row) : · jpeg_size(path) :
      ⚠ ⚠ Since v3552 (REG-1699) READ_PROMPT asks each named item's point (`names_xy`, in the reader's picture - the row's `xySpace` [w, h] - scaled here to the frame) and an equipped item's doll slot (`names_slot`). FORWARD ONLY, his ruling: PROMPT_ANSWERS_SAME_AS keeps p1839's seals and read counts, so rows from before v3552 carry neither field and stay UNPLACED with the reason. `character` is never asked - the name comes from the character-select learner (char_select, REG-1601); a reel no login reaches is `unattributed` with a denominator. ⚠ A login row is ALWAYS a game-session boundary; a reel with no login joins the previous one only across ROLLOVER_GAP_MS (90 s, pinned against the watcher's relook + period + stop) and inherits its character as `carried`. ⚠ Four doll slots are UNMEASURED (slot_identity) and get item+frame but box None with the reason; a frame off the calibrated aspect band (his 1280x756 fixture) likewise. ⚠ The reader's slot WORD and the point's GEOMETRY must agree - a disagreement is a CONFLICT and the item is unplaced with both answers. ⚠ owed is None (UNKNOWN) when no journal or the store cannot be read, never 0. Pure stdlib + slot_identity; the store rides tv_diablo._fixture_root so a fixture world never touches his ledger.
- **inventory_law.py** — The hardcoded three-class item law — LOCKED furniture (Horadric Cube, tomes, Wirt's Leg, keys), CONSUMABLE (potions, scrolls, gold), and LOOT, the only class worth a register.
      ↪ classify · is_locked · is_consumable · worth_registering · main
      ⚠ Deliberately NOT a roster — it word-boundary-matches the words D2R itself prints, because a reader hands over OCR of a tooltip, not a database id. LOCKED is tested BEFORE consumable and the order is load-bearing: a *Scroll* of Town Portal is a consumable while the TOME is furniture, and a reader that saw both on one line must not get the tome demoted. It changes no behaviour — it exists to give the retro readers the answer control_app already had.
- **item_identity.py** — The two vocabularies for one item: what the VAULT calls a physical thing and what the CHRONICLE calls a grail line, separating identity from rendering so a Latent charm never witnesses a renewed one and an apostrophe byte never splits a row in two.
      ↪ fold_rendering · split_name · vault_key · chronicle_key · same_vault_entity · same_chronicle_entry · qualified_forms
      ⚠ Two vocabularies for one item, on one axis: a QUALIFIER (Latent/Renewed) is IDENTITY and never folds; an apostrophe byte, a base-type tail and an unambiguous parenthetical are RENDERING and do. ⚠ The parenthetical is decided by MEASUREMENT, not punctuation — 'Athena's Wrath' is a unique and nothing else so '(set piece)' folds, while 'Crescent Moon' is a unique AND a runeword so '(amulet)' is the only thing separating two real items.
- **item_tables.py** — Generates tv/item_tables.json from the D2R CASC: exactly what tv/d2s_read.py needs to decode a character save - per stat id its item and character-save bit widths, per item code its display name, kind (armor/weapon/misc) and stackable flag, uniques and set items by *ID, and runewords by 1-based runes.txt row. load() returns None, never an empty dict, when it has not been generated.
      ↪ build · load · verify · main
      ⚠ THE STATS BLOCK AND AN ITEM USE DIFFERENT WIDTHS for the same stat: csvBits (character 'gf' section) vs saveBits (item bitstream) - strength is 10 bits in one and 8 in the other, and reading one with the other derails. A table KEY is not a display name: 97 unique keys differ from what the game prints (Thudergod's Vigor is Thundergod's Vigor), so names come from item-names.json / item-runes.json and `namesFrom` says which. runes.txt has no Runeword80, so the row and the RunewordN key disagree from row 80 on; both are stored and neither is picked. Never wrap the pull in the perl alarm idiom (see affix_lexicon).
- **lane_lock.py** — One key from one frame: at most ONE write lane is ever unlocked — the vault surface OR exactly one chronicle ledger — and ambiguity locks both.
      ↪ lane_for · may_write
      ⚠ lane_for() returns None rather than picking when a frame looks like BOTH a stash and a chronicle page — that is the deliberate behaviour, not a failure to decide, because the two wrong answers are not symmetrical (a Chronicle row filed as ownership claims he owns something he only saw listed). A cheap pixel gate is NOT sufficient: measured 2026-08-18, `classify_stash_grid` calls a real Chronicle page 'stash' with frac_dark 0.5364 and 11 dark columns, and only the AI classifier gets it right. The v1999 scar: `personal` and `shared` — the two tabs he is in most — were missing from the tab tuple, so ambiguous frames were not seen as ambiguous and the lock stayed OPEN.
- **menu_run.py** — Alphabetical-order detector: a batch of names ascending against the roster came off a Chronicle MENU page, not out of one of his containers.
      ↪ looks_like_a_menu_page · ascending_fraction · longest_ascending_run
      ⚠ It judges a BATCH and never a name — `looks_like_a_menu_page` refuses anything under MIN_RUN=8, and a per-name verdict derived from it would be superstition. Unknown names are DROPPED, not zeroed. Critically, NO Python calls it: the live intake gate is a JS twin, `window._menuAscendingFraction` in bible.html, and this module is the reference copy whose constants (MIN_RUN 8, STRONG 0.90) test_control.py pins the JS against. A refused batch is DROPPED rather than held, so he cannot see or overturn it.
- **mule_slot_map.py** — #174 v-B: generates the doll-slot table the mule window's picker reads - every base item's slot kind (helm / armor / shield / weapon / gloves / belt / boots / ring / amulet / quiver / carried) from his install's armor/weapons/misc .txt `type` column, plus every unique and set item's base from tv/item_tables.json, plus (#174 v-B review) what a hand can hold beside the other: weapons.txt 2handed / 1or2handed, what a bow shoots (itemtypes.txt Shoots) and which bases one class alone uses (itemtypes.txt Class) - and writes it into bible.html as MULE_BASE_SLOT_SRC / MULE_NAMED_BASE_SRC / MULE_BASE_RULES_SRC. --check compares the block with a fresh pull.
      ↪ build · rules · named · render · embedded · check · write · main
      ⚠ It WRITES bible.html (atomic, refuses when bible.html.EDIT_LOCK is held) between the two ⟦MULE_BASE_SLOT⟧ markers and nowhere else. The slot SUBTYPE (a Shako is a helm, not a torso) exists only in the install's excel `type` column, so --check without an install is 77 = UNKNOWN, never green; CI still proves every name's KIND against the committed tv/item_tables.json. The named-item map is derived from item_tables.json alone and drops, rather than guesses, a name whose bases sit in different slots. The game names "The Stone of Jordan"; the board says "Stone of Jordan" - the page folds case, curly apostrophes and a leading The before looking a name up.
- **read_names_feeder.py** — The ONE caller of read_names_lane.split() that writes. split() has always judged every journal-ring PANEL name through the real gate (vault_retro.gate, 0.55 conf / 2 witnesses) and separated HELD from OWED, and until v3323 nothing consumed autoOwed - its own header said 'the accumulator has no other feeder ... they were never judged'. This module hands each bankable owed name to control_app.vault_apply and invents NO judgement of its own: the door re-gates every row at the write and the board's vaultAccumApply does the landing - dated, merge-max, undoable, the same tick his hand uses. plan() writes nothing and can be read freely.
      ↪ plan · apply · MAX_PER_TICK
      ⚠ ⛔ BANKING IS WHAT RELEASES FOOTAGE. Its sibling read_names_lane.py records why it writes nothing: landing a name lets reel_retention drop the reel's `rows-not-banked` hold, and footage has no un-delete. That is DELIBERATE here and it is the whole point - banking through the board is the legitimate release path - but it is why this module is capped at MAX_PER_TICK=4, asks self_arming.may('vault.apply') and FAILS CLOSED on an unreadable lock, declines any name whose container is UNPLACED rather than guessing a lane, and treats the door's refusal as a RESULT carried back in `why` rather than a swallow. ⚠ IT BANKS NOTHING TODAY: measured at ship 2026-09-18, split() gives autoOwed 0 (names 60, auto 6, manual 54, autoHeld 1 - Crescent Moon, referents UNIQUE *and* RUNEWORD so it can never name one cell). A lane that is ON with lifetime work 0 is the vault_autoreel_tick scar, so control_app._RNF_STATE counts runs/banked LIFETIME and starts owed=None - UNKNOWN, never a confident 0. It ticks in _vault_autoread_loop under its OWN lane name tvd-read-names-feeder, never vault-autoread's: that tick SPENDS on paid sweeps and this one costs nothing, and one supervisor row must not answer for two lanes.
- **read_names_lane.py** — Splits names the DEEP reader put in the journal ring into auto-tallyable (3+ witnesses, conf floor) versus manual-lane, exposing that 119 unbanked names were never REFUSED — they were never judged.
      ↪ split · evidence · report · rosters · referents_of
      ⚠ ⛔ IT WRITES NOTHING, and the reason is a deleter: reel_retention holds a reel with tag `rows-not-banked`, so landing a name in vault_accum.json RELEASES that reel's footage for pruning — wiring 119 unjudged names in would hand a deleter 119 new permissions and footage has no un-delete. Confidence is NOT the blocker (every deep row sits 0.60-0.95 against a 0.55 floor); witnesses are. The three names that DO clear 3 witnesses — Horadric Cube, Tome of Town Portal, Tome of Identify — corroborate only because every character carries them. rosters() refuses to PRODUCE an empty roster and split() refuses to CONSUME one.
- **read_pictures.py** — The ONE answer to 'is the picture of this read on disk, and if not, WHY' - behind the doctor row 'a read left no picture', /api/picture_status (asked by the board's evidence panel before it draws an image), and the recorder's own refusal record.
      ↪ status_for · verdict · who_took · named_reads · record_refusal · budget_spent_mb · locator
      ⚠ It never deletes and never decides to keep: it READS the reapers' tombstones, the recorder's refusal record and the frames on disk, and names who took a read's picture. Measured 2026-09-28: the frames behind String of Ears and seven Chronicle-page reads were gone, taken by three deleters none of which asked whether a frame was evidence. Anything it cannot establish is UNKNOWN, never 'fine' or 0. Under MIN_FREE_GB a read's picture is still saved small (1024 px, q70, 150 MB/hour); below HARD_FLOOR_GB (1 GB) nothing is written and that refusal is recorded here.
- **skill_tables.py** — Generates tv/skill_tables.json from the D2R CASC for the planner's skill-tree panel: per class code (8 on his install - the classic seven and the Warlock) its id, name, icon sheet and three tabs in panel order, each {index, page, name, skills}, every skill {id, key, name, row, col, reqlevel, prereqs (ids), prereqKeys, iconIndex, maxlvl}, plus the 6 x 3 grid and its pixel rows/columns from the HD layout. assemble() is the pure part (a fake install drives it in the law); load() returns None, never an empty dict, when it has not been generated.
      ↪ assemble · build · load · verify · main
      ⚠ A PAGE IS NOT A TAB POSITION: skilldesc SkillPage 3 is the Druid's Elemental, which the panel shows LEFTMOST, and the Warlock's leftmost tab string is SkillCategoryWa3 - so 'tab i = page i+1' and 'read the key's number' are both wrong. The order is derived per class from charstats StrSkillTab1..3 (the page's item modifier) against the layout's textTab0..2, kept only as the unique best agreement, else index/name null with tabWhy. A skills.txt KEY is not a name (Wearwolf is Werewolf; 27 of 240 differ) and a skill with no string is null, never its key. Filter trees on skills.txt charclass: monster skills carry SkillPage too. excel\base\ is the non-RotW copy (its Warlock rows are placeholders) and is not pulled. Never wrap the pull in the perl alarm idiom (see affix_lexicon).

      ⚠ A backup that was never read back is not a restore. Its copies are only as good as the verification that follows them — see the RESTORE work in task #81, which measured that nothing reads these saves back.
- **vault_bank.py** — The one reader for the stash-side bank, reporting what it accumulated and saying out loud when it folded two different wordings of empty into one.
      ↪ state · headline
      ⚠ The ONE reader for the stash-side bank. It folds two different 'empty' wordings and REPORTS that it folded them, so a caller cannot mistake a fold for a measurement.
- **vault_corpus.py** — Indexes which frames of his reels show an ownership surface and which stash tab is active, then fits the 10x4 inventory lattice and reads per-cell occupancy — pixels only, no vision spend.
      ↪ scan · inventory_lattice · inventory_occupancy · inventory_reading · infer_transfer
      ⚠ The loose frames/hist ROOT (883 frames) and the 27 REELS the sweeps walk (1,970 frames) share ZERO filenames — they are separate archives, and every stash measurement before this file was taken on the LOOSE half that no sweep has ever walked. REG-185's '0 of 17 reels declare an ownership surface' never meant 'there is no stash footage', only 'no reel DECLARED one'. The INVENTORY works because 10x4 is KNOWN and ENFORCED — the refusal does the work and the pitch fit only has to agree — so there is deliberately NO stash lattice: RotW's stash dimensions are unrecorded and any stash occupancy would be a guess dressed as a measurement. Gold text alone does not find the title; the tight fraction WINDOW does (lobby, in-game menu and Chronicle panel all print gold). _fit() can return None and callers must not unpack it blind (v2799).
- **vault_evidence.py** — Decides which cleared vault marks come back after a reset, from each item's own looks, where a trial is a distinct visit (never a frame of a still screen): watched under 10 visits, proven near 10 at the Wilson bar, hardened near 20. Names items filed above what their visits earn (retro: WATCHED), and keeps them filed.
      ↪ tier · rebuild_plan · plan_from_ledger · cited_frames · tier_census · pictures_gone · reset_receipt · retro_plan ← POST /api/vault_rebuild_plan (the plan's `retro` field) · rarity_of ← every plan row, tier_census.byRarity, vault_proven_names, evidence_for (#51: magic/rare stand on the same looks) · stand ← control_app.evidence_for, the vault branch (one item's tier + rarity) · rarity_tally_say / rebuilt_rarity_say ← the doctor's evidence-tiers and vault-reset rows
      ⚠ #51 (REG-1519): rarity_of is the ONE resolver for what an item IS - the roster for a unique/set (item_identity._rosters, borrowed), the looks' `quality` vote through vault_retro._quality_of for a rolled name (magic->blue, rare->gold), a tie or a blank is None (UNKNOWN), never white; a name on both a unique and a runeword roster is not decided by the roster. The board's rebuild door reads the plan row's rarity to file blue/gold into the MAGIC & RARE locker. A trial is a distinct VISIT, never a frame (his ruling 2026-09-28, §34.2): _measure counts vault_retro.gate's own look id (witness = session#REOPEN_GAP_MS bucket, else the bare session) folded by vault_retro._fold_bare_sessions, borrowed, never copied. Counting witness ROWS scored Radiance HARDENED 103/103 on 2 visits; corroborate's a-tier-stands-on-its-looks now compares the tier table with the gate. The frame count rides beside as `frames` and no tier reads it. tier() and rebuild_plan() are the only bar, and the bound is confidence.wilson_lower — a second table or a second Wilson function is the drift this file exists to prevent. plan_from_ledger reads a path the caller names and never writes it; a ledger it cannot read is ok:false, not an empty rebuilt list. It does not file. The board's full reset files the plan through window.vaultFile and never reads `retro`. retro_plan names items filed above their visits and keeps them filed — it has no unfile half. Equipped rows and kept charms (sunder, annihilus, torch, gheed) come back below the bar; an unreadable count does not.
- **vault_fixture_reels.py** — Materialises tiny synthetic reels (real JPEGs + index.json, ~30KB per tree) for the vault scenarios, so the suite never again holds gigabytes of his real footage hostage.
      ↪ materialise · signatures_are_distinct · tooltip_pair · hover_reel · cleanup
      ⚠ Every frame must have DISTINCT pixels, not just distinct names: vault_simulate.run passes sig=chronicle_retro.jpeg_sig and the sweep dedupes on that signature, so two byte-similar frames collapse into one and a scenario silently loses a witness — hence `signatures_are_distinct` as a first-class check. It exists because the armed prune deleted 123MB of reels NAMED in vault_simulate.py: the retention rule for 'the test suite opens this by name' globbed only tv/test_*.py, and vault_simulate.py is the module that DEFINES what the tests run. The rule restored is the mirror image of the usual one — live data management must not be able to eat the fixtures.
- **vault_fixture_unsorted.py** — Builds a SYNTHETIC 215-pill unsorted dock from the real bible.html catalogue so the Vault Manager's layout can be designed without touching d2r_owned.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ dock · widest · stresses_the_real_edges · as_localstorage · main
      ⚠ Nothing in the tree imports or runs it — it is a hand-run design fixture. It PROVES itself before being trusted: stresses_the_real_edges() fails the fixture unless it has 50+ names, an 8-character length spread and BOTH apostrophe bytes, because a fixture that renders tidily certifies a layout the real data will break. It falls back to a named hand-written spread when the catalogue cannot be read — never to a silent empty list — and as_localstorage() returns a string it never writes, so aiming a harness at his real profile is the caller's bug to avoid.
- **vault_retro.py** — Retro sweep of sealed reels into an owned / unsure / throwOut vault proposal: merge-max across sessions, two-SESSION witnesses, honest-absent, never writes.
      ↪ sweep · gate · merge_vault · apply_payload · order_reels
      ⚠ Independence is counted in SESSIONS, not still-runs — two still-runs inside one reel are ONE witness looking twice at the same stash, so a single-session reel can never ground a name however confident the reader is. It NEVER writes: `apply_payload()` only shapes a proposal for the one existing apply (window.chronicleApply), which owns the stamp, merge-max and undo. throwOut clears a strictly higher bar on BOTH axes because there is no un-throw in Diablo. All thresholds (STILL_MAX_DIFF, MIN_RUN_FRAMES, the still-run grouping) are imported from chronicle_retro, not restated.
- **vault_simulate.py** — Drives the vault lane end to end on his REAL reels with a scripted reader injected, printing the whole ownership/discard decision in the Vault manager's own words — no vision calls, no writes.
      ↪ run · build · explain · scripted_reader · main
      ⚠ Only the READER's answer is injected — the frame names, timestamps, still-run grouping (via chronicle_retro), the real gate and the real merge are all the production code, because the archive contains everything except what a reader would have said. The rule it exists to make visible: seeing an item MORE often never means throw it away — repetition decides OWNERSHIP, and only the reader's own junk flag can propose a discard, and then only across three separate recordings. So the scenarios deliberately include cases that SHOULD refuse. It calls console_safe.enable() at import because a non-ASCII print on his Windows console crashes the script while reporting a clean tree.
- **vault_speed_bench.py** — V1c (#152 item 4): the hand-add speed CURVE - N hand adds (default 50/200/550) on a fresh whole page in node through the real door (vaultHandAdd) and through his click (vaultHandTake, which repaints after every add), printing per size the total, the mean of the last 10 adds before each mark and one paint. --memo-off sets window._vaultMemoOff = true, which is the path v3784 took, so before and after are measured on one tree.  ·  *no importer*
      ↪ main · measure · BODY
      ⚠ A REPORT, not a gate: a clock moves with the load on the box, so the gate is test_a_hand_add_is_not_quadratic (it counts work). 550 adds with the memos off take 10-20 minutes in node, and node is not Chrome - GrokBot's perf3.py on his screen is the browser measurement.
- **vault_wilson.py** — Sabotage harness for the `vault.apply` lock — six proposals the write door must refuse, Wilson-scored offline and again against the running console.
      ↪ score · score_live · forget_note · bank_into_proof_queue · main
      ⚠ It never applies anything: every row carries an EMPTY evidence list, so the v1595 re-gate refuses before the board is ever asked. Banking is OPT-IN (`--bank`) because an audit that writes evidence is not an audit — and there are TWO bank doors, the shared `bank_into_proof_queue` and a separate LIVE-witness bank in main(); guarding only the first still moved his ledger. `vault.forget` is UNPROVABLE by construction (no refusal path) and `forget_note()` says so rather than leaving a blank.
- **vault_witness.py** — Holds the vault's STORED owned rows beside a re-gate of their own witnesses at today's live bars, and publishes the disagreement.
      ↪ verdict · stored · regate · report
      ⚠ Nothing in the console, heart or doctor calls it — only its own gate imports it and store_owners names it in a prose registry string, so the reading it produces reaches no surface. The hole it watches is real: control_app.vault_apply's re-gate lives inside `if caller_supplied` and the console button posts `{}`, so a result gated under an older bar applies as-is (6 of 7 rows, measured). AGE is context, never a verdict — the verdict comes only from the re-gate disagreeing.
- **write_witness.py** — Runtime witness that patches open/io.open/os.replace to RECORD which module actually writes each watched reel store (vault_swept, vault_accum, reel_tombstones, retro_triage, chron_evidence).
      ↪ watching · Witness · writers · report
      ⚠ It ONLY observes — it records and calls straight through to the originals, never redirecting, blocking or fabricating a write. ⚠ And it PROVES NOTHING UNTIL SOMETHING RUNS under `with watching()`: enabling it does not gather evidence, so the per-store answer is a measurement nobody has taken yet — which is not the same fact as 'one writer'. It exists because two static detectors (filename-adjacency grep, AST constant-resolution) both returned ZERO writers for all four stores, measuring the instruments rather than the code. Related trap recorded in write_census.py: patching only `open` also yields 0 — this codebase writes via io.open.
